# S2E22 — "St. Joseph" — Showrunner plan (**revision 1, 2026-11-03**; **Episode Architect's §2B blueprint added 2026-11-03 — §2B wins**; built on the real `saves/after-s2e21.json` and the published `episodes/s2e21.json`; MAIN TIMELINE — `hard-saying/fork/` not read and not applied; the Episode Architect's §2B, when written below, wins on any conflict of ids, slugs and mechanics)

> Writers' room only. 🔒 = hidden canon: plant it, never surface it in a cue, key, previously, logline, nextTime or choice text.
> **THE JOURNAL IS RETIRED (player, 2026-10-30).** **No `journal` array or journal card in any scene, choice, blueprint or brief of this episode.** Key lines ("Story so far") stay and carry the recap. Every key is a complete, spoiler-safe sentence in the player's second person.
> **S2E21 is PLAYED.** Every E21 read is **flat** on the played value (§12). The Architect still gates defensively on the real flag names (exhaustive if/else, never nested).
> **Process:** the notes session is COMPLETE. Player after S2E21: **"Finished, ready for the next one!"** (brief "After S2E21"; no new directives). Every downstream agent, Assembly included, runs its full step. Nobody pauses for notes.
> **What this episode is:** the week he turns nineteen, and the week somebody else does the remembering. **Monday** her step is empty at ten to seven, with a Commons coffee on his and a note under it in her capitals: *FRIDAY'S MINE.* Hale lets the table say *Thursday* again and tells it he has already chosen his opponent for October. A box from Youngstown turns up at the Delgado desk addressed to Miss Lena Broussard in his grandmother's hand. **Tuesday** Fenner follows up for the first time in recorded history, and Skye is ready for him; that night Lena is in the Anchor's walk-in with Rudy and a sack of flour, and Laney has found out about Kevin from somebody who isn't Jack. **Wednesday** is St. Patrick's, and Skye comes to Low Tide straight out of the water — wetsuit peeled to the waist, salt in her hair, the only green she owns — to report that she has questioned the beauty of the sea, as Augustine told her to, for two hours, and it didn't say a word. He brings his proofs. He tells Lena the same evening, through a kitchen door with a KEEP OUT sign on it, and she goes polite on him, which is worse than anything. **Thursday** the Stations come a day early because the cross can wait one day for a saint, and at one minute past midnight she is on his step with flour in her hair. **Friday**, in the middle of Lent, the vestments are white and the Gloria comes back for one day; in the Fishbowl there is a three-tier altar for St. Joseph with his own holy card at the top, bread in the shape of Ohio, fava beans from Nonna and fig cookies from Celeste — and on a side table, where no New Orleans altar would put it, his mother's wedding soup. *That's how much I like you, Costigan.* At twenty past eight Trumbull calls. **Sunday** the statues are veiled, the Gospel is the woman nobody threw a stone at, and that night the House votes and nobody in the Fishbowl is happy about it, for opposite reasons.
> **Binding inputs, in order of precedence:** brief (newest last). **"After S2E21" (NEWEST):** "Finished, ready for the next one!" — no new notes; every standing entry holds. **"After S2E20" (binding in full):** the Catechism campaign — **from the beginning, in order, one section or theme per session duel, here for the whole thing; track it** · Lena learned first and set the condition · **Skye's dressing strategy: a new idea each session, obvious, funny, never touches him** · **CORRECTION: Jack does NOT tell Skye the condition until much later, after several meetings (S2E22 at the earliest; the room places it); the reveal is the player's verbatim scene** — **this plan HOLDS it (§6.8)**. **"SKYE'S BREAKUP AND AFTER" steps 6 and 8 + the mechanic** (each session a duel vs Skye, scored rigor/wit/heart; **she wins some**; distinct register; real philosophy both sides; never a strawman; Jack doesn't always win; counters `skye_cat_jack` / `skye_cat_skye`). **"Lena won't joke about Skye yet"** (a live fear: careful, quiet or sharp; **never funny**). **"Skye and Jack: keep the whole mix"** (sex appeal + intellect + wit/play + fun, every episode she's in) **+ CORRECTION (not lonely; he wants to be with her; learning her body and mind)**. **"Season 2 back-half decisions"** — Skye's long arc to friendship by the end of the semester (still part of her fight; never joins the group; Lena's dislike honest; nothing physical; never at Lena's expense); the Flannery biopic later. **"After S2E17"** — Theo's off-page call with Skye stays hidden. **"After S2E12"** — first "I love you" is a SUMMER 2010 visit (S3): **no "love" between them on any path**; official at the Easter Vigil (S2E24); the Easter dinner holds the alliance reveal and the champagne. **CORRECTION "the first kiss"** — kissing frequently; **heat VERY slow; kissing only; no making out until the summer visits.** **"Lent 2010 observance"** — separate practices (Lena: Friday bread and water, the Stations, off Facebook; Jack: no cigarettes — the sealed pack — and the Friday fast); **dates avoid Lenten Fridays** — the birthday is **a feast with everyone, not a date**. **"Birthdays get celebrated" (standing)** — **Jack, Mar 19 (St. Joseph)**, on the calendar since the S2 pass. **The liturgical-living entry** — Lena's New Orleans St. Joseph's altars on Mar 19; Jack's Youngstown Italian St. Joseph's table — "they half-rediscover in books"; **never say "liturgical living."** **"After S2E10"** — **Kevin is NOT in the alliance.** **"Faith tests: bent, never broken."** Standing hard rules: frank, literary heat, never pornographic mechanics; everyone 18+; **no sex for Jack or Lena with anyone**; no procedural consent beats unless a scene would otherwise read as assault; **never mock the faith; no strawmen; Jack and Lena never die.** Then the **player notes for this episode** (the task, §1). Then bible §23.2 S2E22 (+ the S2E21 hand-off) / §21 / §10.2 "THE CATECHISM CAMPAIGN — DESIGN OF RECORD" / rows #92–#93 / §7, §8.x, §10.8, §10.9 (S2E22 notes added this pass). Then room.md (tics #1–#299) and the period sheet `period/s2e22-mar15-21-2010.md` (**written in parallel this run; read and reconciled in §15.1** — where it conflicts with this plan's liturgical ruling, Canon 1251 governs).

## 0. DATES, SHAPE, TARGET
- **Mon Mar 15, 2010 (≈6:50 a.m. PDT, the east stairwell) – Sun Mar 21 (≈9:20 p.m. PDT, her step).** Title card: **"Monday, March 15 – Sunday, March 21, 2010"**. Title: **"St. Joseph"** (the bible map's title).
- **Liturgy (Researcher confirms NAB wording; §15):** Mon Mar 15 – Thu Mar 18: Lenten weekdays of the Fourth Week (Mon John 4:43–54; Tue John 5:1–16, the pool at Bethesda; Wed John 5:17–30; Thu John 5:31–47 — none on the page). **Wed Mar 17 is St. Patrick's Day** (an optional memorial in the US, a Lenten weekday — no dispensation needed: it's not a Friday). **Fri Mar 19: the SOLEMNITY OF ST. JOSEPH, Spouse of the Blessed Virgin Mary** — **not transferred in 2010** (it moves only when Mar 19 falls on a Sunday of Lent or in Holy Week) [R-confirm]: **white vestments in the middle of Lent; the Gloria sung (the first since Ash Wednesday); the Creed**; readings 2 Sam 7:4–5a, 12–14a, 16 · Ps 89 ("The son of David will live for ever") · Rom 4:13, 16–18, 22 ("hoping against hope") · **Matt 1:16, 18–21, 24a** (Joseph, a righteous man; "do not be afraid"; he did as the angel commanded) [alt. Luke 2:41–51a]. **Fri Mar 19 is also the fifth Friday of Lent** (Feb 19, 26, Mar 5, 12, **19**). **Sat Mar 20:** John 7:40–53 (not on the page). **Sun Mar 21, FIFTH SUNDAY OF LENT, Year C:** Isa 43:16–21 ("See, I am doing something new!") · Ps 126 · Phil 3:8–14 ("forgetting what lies behind but straining forward") · **John 8:1–11** (the woman caught in adultery; he writes on the ground; "Let the one among you who is without sin be the first to throw a stone"; "Neither do I condemn you. Go, and from now on do not sin any more."). **Crosses and statues veiled in purple from this Sunday** at St. Brendan's — **only if** the Researcher confirms the US rubric in force in 2010 (§15.1); otherwise s20 drops the veiling.
- **THE SOLEMNITY AND THE FRIDAY — the ruling (verify and use correctly):** **Canon 1251**: abstinence from meat is observed on all Fridays **unless a solemnity falls on a Friday**. St. Joseph on Fri Mar 19, 2010 **lifts the Friday abstinence**; the Church's only Lenten *fast* days are Ash Wednesday and Good Friday, so there is no obligatory fast to lift. **Jack's Friday fast and Lena's bread and water are voluntary**; a solemnity is a feast and not a penitential day, so both set them down for the day — and say so plainly, once (Lena, who checked it twice and asked a priest: "Canon twelve fifty-one."). **The New Orleans altar stays meatless anyway, by custom** (the altars were built in Lent and the custom kept the Lenten table; the food is given to the poor) [R] — so the **meat is NOT on the altar**: it is Ma's wedding soup on a side table, and that is the point of "That's how much I like you, Costigan." (§5.6). **Nobody on the page calls meat on this Friday a sin, and nobody calls the altar's meatlessness an obligation.** The Stations: **Osei moves the fifth Friday's Stations to Thursday night** ("Tomorrow is a solemnity. The cross will wait one day; it is used to waiting.") — a pastoral choice, fiction governs [R: plausibility only].
- **Lent, frankly:** Jack is on **day twenty-seven (Mon) to day thirty-three (Sun)** without a cigarette (day 20 = Mon Mar 8, as published; the birthday is **day thirty-one**). The sealed pack (Reds) rides in the Carhartt's breast pocket all week; its one paragraph is **Friday night** — the solemnity's loophole offered (Theo, a birthday cigar on the loading-dock steps, or Danny's "You smoking?") and declined; **the pack stays sealed on every path** (its fate is Easter, into her tin — HELD). Lena's Facebook stays dark (she cannot see his birthday wall; Laney can). Lena's bread and water on Friday is **set down for the solemnity**; she keeps the Thursday Stations.

- **Reading the save (the Showrunner's note to every writer; never on the page):** **Sincerity 267, Candor 160** — this player tells people things: he told Lena first, told Kevin the lighthouse wasn't nothing, told Osei he still loves Skye and didn't pretend otherwise, told Lena she'd had the room at a minute forty, told her the socks weren't hard, and told her, under the olive tree, that the Costigans haven't kept the day since Danny went away. **He conceded the first round to Skye because it was true, and then beat her with the two arguments he believes** (the billboard; the line that wants something back). He keeps faith with the absent and is honest about the present. So: **this week the remembering is done *to* him.** He has spent three episodes planning things for Lena; now she plans one for him, and the episode's tenderness is in watching him receive it — badly at first (he didn't want a fuss), then not badly at all. On Wednesday he will be honest with Skye in a way that costs him (§6.4 round 3), and honest with Lena the same evening in a way that costs her (§7). On Friday his brother calls. None of the options make light of Skye, of Lena's fear, or of Danny; none says "love" to Lena or about her.

### ACT ONE — "FRIDAY'S MINE" (Mon Mar 15 – Tue Mar 16) · ≈4,150 per path
- **s01 · `MONDAY · 6:50 AM · DELGADO HALL, THE EAST STAIRWELL — FRIDAY'S MINE`** · ≈700 · ♥ J/L · no music · set `{"s2e22_on":true,"lena_note":true}` · choice `s2e22_note` (no key, no cue) · *the roles reversed again.* Day twenty-seven. Return address Glenwood Avenue. Marcus won't give it to him ("It says Broussard, man."). It rattles: dried beans. Lena arrives from the stairwell at a dead run, takes it, holds it behind her back: "You didn't see this." — "It's from my grandmother." — "It's from *Nonna*. That's a different thing." She goes up two at a time. (**Ma told Nonna; Nonna mailed the fave to the girl — "*Lei lo sa?*" answered by post.** Knowledge: Lena called Ma Monday morning, §13 — Ma says so on Friday, not here.) Ends on Marcus, who has watched all of it, saying "Your grandma spells it with a T," and Jack laughing in the lobby.

BRENDAN'S PARISH HALL — THE LINE`** · ≈900 · ♥ J/L + the Saturday-meal regulars (walk-ons, uncast; one named by Lena) · no music · set `{"parish_meal":true,"rel.lena.warmth":"+1","soul.grace":"+1"}` · choice `s2e22_line` (no key, no cue) · → s20 · *Dorothy Day would've.* Lena on the serving line is a different woman again — the Catholic Worker in her (bible §7): she knows the regulars' names; she gives a man named Lyle the lucky-bean speech and a bean, and he puts it in his shirt pocket and buttons the pocket; she argues with a woman about whether St. Joseph or St. Anthony finds lost things (Anthony; she loses). Jack sees her in her element and is quiet about it. In the parish hall kitchen, over the dish sink, she kisses him with her hands wet and doesn't dry them first. `s2e22_line`: [Sincerity] "You're good at this." — "It's not a talent. It's a habit." (Aristotle, Book Two, from the bowling alley — callback without the word) · [Wit] "Lyle's going to win the lottery now." · [Restraint] *Wash. Let her talk to Lyle.*
- **s19h · [Hale] `SATURDAY · 11:30 AM · HOLLIS HALL, 314 — PATRON OF A HAPPY DEATH`** · ≈900 · Hale two-hander · no music · set `{"hale_cuccidati":true,"rel.hale.warmth":"+1","rel.hale.trust":"+1"}` · choice `s2e22_hale` (no key, no cue) · → s20 · *I intend to keep her for both.* Hale in his office on a Saturday, a stack of 109H essays and a fountain pen, the radiator, one window. He takes the napkin of fig cookies "from the opposition" with real pleasure and eats one at once. **Possessive, honest, funny:** "Miss Broussard is my best student and my worst defeat, and I intend to keep her for both." He's heard it was Jack's birthday (from the Fishbowl's noise: the whole building heard Louis Prima) — "Nineteen. A dreadful age. I was insufferable at nineteen. I'm told I've improved." St. Joseph: **"Patron of a happy death. My father would have liked him."** — 🔒 one clause, no gloss (the vicar; the serenity; the mother — never said). [`s2e21_ohio=="candor"`] "And the parked car. Was it accepted?" — "It's being argued. Wednesdays." — Hale, an eyebrow, and lets it go (he does not learn more). `s2e22_hale`: [Wit] / [Sincerity] / [Candor] — the Architect sets them on Hale's question "Why did she send them?" (*Because she's kind* / *Because she won* / *Because she wants a rematch*). 🔒 Nora, the postcard, the forty-one seconds, Compline: nothing.
- **s20 · `SUNDAY · 11:30 AM · ST. BRENDAN THE NAVIGATOR — VEILED`** · ≈850 · ♥ J/L (one pew) + Osei, Gus, Kevin (and **Laney beside him** — her organizing reaches the pew), Clare (the loft) · no music field · set `{"mass_mar21":true,"soul.grace":"+1","resolve.jack":"+1","resolve.lena":5}` · `next: s21` · *he wrote on the ground.* The crucifix and the statues **veiled in purple** (the altar society, Saturday; Joseph's statue too, two days after his feast — Jack notices that one); the white is gone, the purple is back, Lent's last stretch. Isaiah's "something new"; **John 8**: the woman, the stones, **he bends and writes on the ground with his finger** — and they go away one by one, beginning with the elders. **Osei's homily, two sentences** — e.g., *"We are not told what he wrote in the dirt. I have always thought each of them read his own name there, and that is why the old ones left first."* (Jerome's tradition, softened [R]; never aimed.) 🔒 Jack thinks — one clause — of Trumbull, and then he is listening again (the elision rule: the narration declines the memory). At the sign of peace — not last week's grease pencil (tic #280) — something new: a burn-blister on her thumb from Rudy's fryer; he sees it; she sees him see it.
- **s21 · `SUNDAY · 7:35 PM · DELGADO HALL, THE FISHBOWL — THE VOTE`** · ≈1,300 · ♥ J/L (direct opposition as sport) + Margot (for the *Tide*), Theo (a book on the count), Laney, Kevin, Caleb and Yusuf (in from Hollis to watch — optional), the Fishbowl's TV on C-SPAN · no music · set `{"vote_night":true}` · choice `s2e22_vote` (**key every option**; no cue) · *told you* — **§8 binds.**
- **s22 · `SUNDAY · 9:20 PM · DELGADO HALL, HER STEP — SOMETHING NEW`** · ≈450 · ♥ J/L · no music · set `{"rel.lena.trust":"+1"}` · **`end: true`** (no choice) · *straining forward.* After the vote, on her step. Frank's text arrives (§8.4). She's still angry; she leans back against his shins anyway. ¶39 open on his knee (**"How Can We Speak about God?"** — the next Wednesday's) and the Ohio bread, a heel of it, in a napkin between them, which she breaks and gives him half of. Ends on her — her voice or her face — not on an object (tic #275): e.g., she reads ¶39's first line aloud over his shoulder, unasked, in her lector's voice and then in her own, and says, "Wednesday," and nothing after it, and the word has nothing in it but the day (never a joke; never a crack; she has said it as a fact for two weeks and means it).

### THE ROMANCE LADDER, THIS EPISODE (Jack/Lena — the Voice Editor checks it; each step named against the last; **kissing only**)
1. **s01** — the gift reversed: *FRIDAY'S MINE.* (his word, her capitals); a quick kiss over a blank legal pad.
2. **s05** — a floury hand flat on his chest; "You didn't see me." — kissing as conspiracy.
3. **s08** — **the first hurt between them since the levee that isn't a fight**: formal, polite; he names it or waits; she comes back (§7). The kiss there is the coming-back, not a reward.
4. **s12** — midnight: the first kiss of nineteen, hers, sugar and anise; she won't stay (dough rising) — the first time she *chooses* a thing for him over a minute with him, and he loves her for it (narration: never "love").
5. **s14** — white lace; "That was kind of her."; a zeppola at noon on a Friday.
6. **s15** — the altar: he receives it; "That's how much I like you, Costigan."; in front of everyone, nothing more than her hand on his sleeve and his face — the ensemble sees it (the alliance's terror of jinxing it; Laney's attempted casting).
7. **s16** — she speaks to his brother (on [Sincerity]); she hears "That was always me." and doesn't ask.
8. **s17** — Mémère's words; who told her; "It was very hard."; **the longest kiss of the week** and **the episode's only "he wants her" paragraph** (tic #274) — and the line held, gladly; heat +1, no making out.
9. **s19l** [lena] — wet hands, the dish sink · or **s20** — the blister on her thumb at the sign of peace.
10. **s21–s22** — opposition as sport, furious at the same Congressman for opposite reasons; one kiss to end an argument neither has won; her back against his shins.

### THE SKYE LADDER, THIS EPISODE (Jack/Skye — the whole mix every scene she's in: intellect · wit/play · sex appeal · fun · one dropped voice per session)
1. **s04** — Fenner follows up; the pilings; "I was wrong about him. I hate that."; the A; "Bring the good ones."
2. **s07** — out of the water; the only green she owns; "Last week was wardrobe."; the surfers talk swell with her; [`s2e21_gown=="wit"`] "Now who's underdressed?"; [`s2e21_c3=="reframe"`] she turned it over till Friday — and Saturday.
3. **s07a–s07c** — the sea said nothing; Debbie's voice (**the dropped voice of the session, round 2**); "So where's Chapter Two?"
4. **s07d** — the verdict; "Bring adjectives."; out the back door to the bike path with the board, barefoot, and doesn't look back (a different exit from the gown's: no heels, no doorframe — tic list "the same exit gesture").
5. **s14** — her text: she did not post on his wall, and says so.
6. **Never:** a touch (she never; he never), a lean toward a kiss, anything about Lena, anything about a promise. **She doesn't know about Friday's altar.**

## 2B. BLUEPRINT (Episode Architect — final scene ids, slugs, music, sets, beats, choice maps, inserts) — PRE-PRODUCTION, 2026-11-03, built on the real `saves/after-s2e21.json` (S2E21 played; read flat — every flag below re-checked against the save this pass), the published `episodes/s2e21.json` (every previously quote and callback re-checked verbatim this pass), `s2e10.json` (Danny's birthday call — the recorded announcement's register), `s1e20.json`, all 230 published `music` fields S1E01–S2E21 (grepped this pass), the context pack `drafts/s2e22_pack.md`, the period sheet `writers/period/s2e22-mar15-21-2010.md` (**not yet extended** with plan §15's [R] items — §2B.2 rules or hedges every one), the brief "### After S2E07" to the end (read in the pack's §1.3 and its exact-text entries, through **"After S2E21"**), `continuity.md` (Kevin: San Jose; his mother a Walgreens pharmacist; the insulin) and the schema `writers/schema.md`

### 2B.0 Conventions and the Architect's changes (read first)

**Where §2B and anything else in this file differ, §2B wins.** §0–§2, §3, §5–§8 and §11–§16 are the Showrunner's plan (kept). **§4.1, §10, and §11's key/cue/resolve lists are superseded by §2B** (final as written to `drafts/s2e22_header.json` and the per-chunk briefs `drafts/s2e22_brief_act1.md`–`act5.md`). §5 (the birthday), §6 (session two), §7 (Wednesday's telling) and §8 (the vote) bind in full **except where §2B names a correction** (listed below).

**THE JOURNAL IS RETIRED (player, 2026-10-30).** No `journal` key in any scene, choice or response of S2E22. **(no journal: retired)** stands wherever an earlier blueprint had a journal table. Key lines carry the recap: each is a complete, spoiler-safe sentence in the player's second person, past tense.

**PROCESS: the notes session with the player is COMPLETE** (player after S2E21: "Finished, ready for the next one!"). Every downstream agent, Assembly included, runs its full step. **MAIN TIMELINE** — `hard-saying/fork/` is not read and not applied.

**THE FOUR RULES THIS EPISODE LIVES OR DIES BY (every Writer, every Editor):**
1. **SKYE DOES NOT KNOW LENA'S CONDITION EXISTS, AND NOTHING TESTS IT.** No line in s04, s07–s07d or s14 — Skye's, Jack's in her hearing, or the narration's while she is on the page — mentions or winks at a promise, terms, a condition, Lena's rules, "not allowed," "a move," or "if you…". She never touches him; he never touches her; she never leans toward a kiss. **"I can work with that" appears nowhere in S2E22**, in any mouth, key, cue, previously, logline or nextTime. The reveal scene (the player's verbatim lines, plan §6.8) is **HELD** — the room recommends after Easter, session 5 or later.
2. **THE BIRTHDAY IS HERS, AND IT IS A FEAST, NOT A DATE.** Lena plans it; Jack receives it. **“That’s how much I like you, Costigan.”** is said in s15 on every path, **before** the choice, verbatim. The solemnity rule is stated **once**, correctly (s14, Lena): Canon 1251 lifts the Friday abstinence; the altar stays meatless by custom; the meat is the soup on the side table.
3. **NO "LOVE" BETWEEN JACK AND LENA, ON ANY PATH** (and no "girlfriend," "boyfriend," "official"). Danny may say “Love you, Professor.” — brothers. **Kissing only; no making out.**
4. **DANNY'S “That was always me. You were the baby.” IS NEVER GLOSSED** (s16, every path, before the choice). The narration declines the memory; the next sentence is about something else. Ma's laundry-basket line (s13) is a memory and is not glossed either. **No narration anywhere says or implies what happened in Struthers** (the elision rule, pack §4.2).

**Format of a choice line:** `[Tag] “Text”` (or an italic action line) · **set** `{…}` · **cue** · **key** · → goto · *response gist*. Quoted choice text is on-screen text (writers may polish wording, never meaning). **Every choice's `set` stores its own flag** (`"s2e22_x":"value"`), written out in full below; do not drop it. Anything in `[ … ]` is the writer's brief, not final prose; everything else in quotation marks in §2B is final and is pasted (writers may polish a word for rhythm, never the idea). **Lines that are verbatim and may not be polished at all:** **“That’s how much I like you, Costigan.”** (s15) · **“It’s a solemnity. The Church says I may.”** / **“It doesn’t say I have to.”** (s15) · **“That was kind of her.”** (s14) · **“Canon twelve fifty-one. I checked it twice and I asked a priest.”** (s14) · **“Thank you for telling me.”** (s08) · **“Was she good?”** — **“Yes.”** — **“Good.”** (s08) · **“She kept her hands to herself.”** (s08, Jack) · **“Who played Joseph?”** … **“That was always me. You were the baby.”** (s16, Danny) · **“Love you, Professor. Happy birthday.”** (s16, Danny — the one sanctioned line repeated under every option; see s16) · **“I did.”** (s17) · **“I’ll have you know this was very hard.”** (s17, on `s2e21_hour=="sincerity"`) · **“Be careful with her, Costigan. Not for you.”** ([theo] s09t) · **“I’m in this room. I’m in it a lot, actually.”** ([kevin] s09k) · **“Bring adjectives.”** (s07d; the nextTime) · **“Absolutely not.”** (s15, Lena) · **“I’ve done the reading.”** (s15, Margot). **Every insert (`sms`, `doc`) in §2B is final:** paste it; invent no other insert. **The only Skye inserts in the episode are s14's three texts.**

**Variables.** Schema variables only; `rel.*` only on the tracked ids (`lena theo laney margot kevin osei hale julian evan becca wes skye`) — **never** `rel.gloria / rudy / frank / maria / nonna / gina / danny / fenner / caleb / yusuf / gus / clare / marcus / odile / celeste`. Traits and soul `"+1"`; `rel.*` ±1 (**no single scene over ±2 on one axis, scene set and choice combined** — checked below); `resolve.jack` as `"+N"` / `"-N"` strings; **`resolve.lena` the absolute number `5`** in the scene `set` of s11, s14, s17, s20 only (pinned). **One duel:** `cat2` (s07a–s07d; §2B.6a; `duel.cat2.rigor / wit / heart` from Jack's moves; **`duel.cat2.skye` writer-set on s07b**). **Campaign counters (long-arc, unprefixed — registry #92):** `skye_cat_jack`, `skye_cat_skye` (ints, `"+1"`), **`cat_section`** (int, **absolute `2`** at s07d), `cat_sessions` (int, `"+1"` at s07d → 2). **Never shown or named on the page.**

**Scoring rule (carried from S2E02–E21):** Tag → trait: Sincerity → `trait.sincerity`, Wit → `trait.wit`, Candor → `trait.candor`, Restraint → `trait.control`. **Place/fork tags** (`Theo`, `Kevin`, `Lena`, `Hale`) pay no trait. **Duel move tags** (`Press`, `Reframe`, `Riposte`, `Concede`, `Personal`, and **s07c's duel `Sincerity`**) pay duel scores and counters and **no trait**. **`Concede` at s21 (the vote, not a duel) pays `rel.lena.trust +1` and no trait.** `soul.honesty +1` on a Candor option **only where the line is a hard truth told**: s08 [Candor] (he names her hurt), s16 [Candor] (he tells Danny what Lena did). `soul.restraint` moves only on choices about desire or the body — **in S2E22 only at s07 [Restraint] (eyes to the page)**. `soul.grace` on prayer, the Mass and the altar: s11, s14, s15, s20 scene sets; [lena] s19l scene set.

**Engine facts relied on (validator-checked):** a scene `set` applies **before** the body renders and before choice `if`s are evaluated — **so no scene's body may gate on a flag its own `set` writes** (s09t gates on `s2e21_wed`, not `theo_knows_cat`, for exactly this reason); a choice's `set` applies before its response renders; `next` cannot read flags; **one choice set per scene, at the scene's end — nothing in the body may follow a choice**; a scene has `choices` **or** `next` **or** `end` (the `end` scene carries no choice); **every scene with choices has at least one option with no `if`** (no option in S2E22 carries an `if`); response items may be strings, inserts or `{"if","text","else"}` objects; inserts may carry `if`; string flags are truthy when set; unknown vars read 0/false; `&&` binds tighter than `||`, **no parentheses**; resolve clamps 0–10. **A body paragraph cannot set state, and a scene `set` cannot be conditional** — branch-dependent flags are set on the choice where the branch is decided (fork scenes carry their own scene `set`, because only that branch enters them).

**Assembly** refuses duplicate scene ids across drafting files — **every scene id below belongs to exactly one drafting chunk (§2B.8).**

**Conditionals.** (a) **S2E21 and everything earlier is PLAYED**: read flat; every earlier-episode read is still written defensively (if/else or exhaustive on the real flag name; the first branch is the played path) — §2B.4 lists every one. (b) **S2E22's own choices** gate later S2E22 lines; every such gate is exhaustive or an `if`/`else` pair, **never nested**. (c) The Wednesday fork is read on the string **`s2e22_wed`** (`theo` / `kevin`); the Saturday fork on **`s2e22_sat`** (`lena` / `hale`) — the scene flags `theo_knows_cat` / `kevin_420` / `parish_meal` / `hale_cuccidati` are equivalent; **gate on the string** so a reader sees one name. (Neither fork is read later in S2E22: both converge without a callback.)

**Texts and clocks.** `{"type":"sms","from":"…","text":"…","time":"…"}`. **PDT all week** (DST began Sun Mar 14). **Ohio is three hours ahead — said exactly once, at s13 ("9:41 Eastern"; #201).** No Eastern clock in s16 (Danny) or s22 (Frank). Sender labels as published: **Jack** (lowercase, short, no apostrophes), **Lena** (full punctuation, "—L." — none this episode; her note is a `doc`), **Dad** for Frank (ALL CAPS), **Gina** (lowercase / CAPITALS when serious), **Theo** (lowercase), **SKYE (MISFIT)** (the name in his RAZR since S1, never changed; no gloss). **No typing indicators, read receipts, front cameras, "selfie," emoji, "viral," "trending," "DM," "ghosted," "closure," "Uber," "foodie," "liturgical living." No lyric quoted from any needle-drop or hymn beyond two lines of Caswall's public-domain *Stabat Mater* (s11) and the Gloria's opening Latin or English phrase (s14); titles only.**

**Real people:** Augustine (*Sermon* 241; *Confessions*), Aquinas (the third way, paraphrased), Pascal, C. S. Lewis ("Bulverism," paraphrased; Jack may name Lewis), Vatican I and Pius XII as the Catechism cites them (≤1 Catechism sentence a round [R]), Waugh (*Brideshead* Book One, paraphrase or one short phrase), Stanislavski (named only), Louis Prima (the needle-drop), Stupak / the Speaker / the President (named only, never quoted, no private conduct). **Low Tide, the Sigma Rho house, St. Brendan's, the Anchor, Delgado and Marisol are fictional; "Sands" is Skye's (fictional) break.** Nobody knows anything after Sun Mar 21 evening.

**Ages (all adults):** Jack 18 (19 from 12:00 a.m. Fri Mar 19), Lena 18, Skye 19, Theo 19, Laney 19, Kevin 19, Margot 19, Clare 19, Caleb, Yusuf, Marcus, the Low Tide barista, the surfers, the Sigma Rho crowd, the Ferrer kid, the RAs, the Newman soprano, Lyle and the Saturday regulars — adults. **Alcohol:** [theo] s09t's green beer (a frat party; Jack's one, on [Wit], or held) — nowhere else; no alcohol at the altar. The Veuve stays corked (unmentioned).

### 2B.2 Period guardrails specific to this blueprint (the prose leans only on the safe wording given here)
- **Calendar.** Mon Mar 15 – Sun Mar 21, 2010, **PDT**. **Sunrise ≈7:05, sunset ≈7:10** (never stated as a number). Jack's Lent: **day twenty-seven (Mon) → day thirty-three (Sun)**; **day thirty-one is the birthday**. The sealed pack (Marlboro Reds) rides in the Carhartt's breast pocket; it is named once, at s16. **Fri Mar 19 = the fifth Friday of Lent AND the Solemnity of St. Joseph.**
- **THE SOLEMNITY AND THE FRIDAY (ruling; plan §0; pack §8):** **Canon 1251** — *abstinence from meat … is to be observed on all Fridays, unless a solemnity should fall on a Friday* [the Code's wording; the page never quotes it]. **No dispensation is involved** (the period sheet's "by dispensation" is WRONG — struck). The only Lenten fast days are Ash Wednesday and Good Friday; **Jack's Friday fast (`jack_fridays`) and Lena's bread and water are voluntary**, and both set them down for the solemnity — said **once**, by Lena, s14: **“No fast today, Costigan. It’s a solemnity. Canon twelve fifty-one. I checked it twice and I asked a priest.”** **The New Orleans altar stays meatless by custom** (it was a Lenten table before it was a feast; the food goes to the poor) — **the meat is Ma's wedding soup on a side table**, s15. **Nobody on the page calls meat on this Friday a sin, or the altar's meatlessness an obligation.** Osei moves the fifth Friday's Stations to Thursday (pastoral choice; fiction governs).
- **Liturgy.** **Fri: white vestments; the Gloria (the first since Lent began); the Creed;** Matthew 1:16, 18–21, 24a (Joseph, a righteous man, unwilling to expose her to shame; "do not be afraid"; he did as the angel commanded) — paraphrase; ≤one phrase quoted. **Sun Mar 21, Fifth Sunday of Lent, Year C: John 8:1–11** (the woman; he bends and writes on the ground; "Let the one among you who is without sin be the first to throw a stone"; they go away one by one, beginning with the elders) — ≤one phrase quoted in s20; the end card carries 8:11. **Purple; no Gloria.** Isaiah 43's "something new" may be named (s22's slug). Weekday readings never on the page.
- **Moon.** New moon Mon Mar 15. **Thu night (s11) a thin crescent going down over the hills after the Stations**; **Fri a thin crescent setting ≈9 p.m.** — s17 (11:48 p.m.) has **no moon in the window and does not say "no moon"** (say nothing). Never "new moon" on the page.
- **Tides (s04):** spring tides around the new moon; **"Monday evening's low, the lowest of the month"** [R]; **never "minus tide"** unless the Researcher confirms it for Mon Mar 15 at a central-coast station.
- **Equinox:** Sat Mar 20, **10:32 a.m. PDT** — Margot, once, s18 ("Spring starts at ten thirty-two. I have it in writing.").
- **The news [R; public record only; no real person quoted; no strawmen]:** Wed (s06): the Catholic Health Association (hospitals) for the Senate bill; the bishops (USCCB) against — "in the paper," paraphrased. **Sun (s21):** Stupak's bloc announced Sunday afternoon (Eastern) they would vote yes after the President agreed to an executive order on abortion funding; **the House passed the Senate bill 219 to 212 at ≈7:45 p.m. PDT** (the reconciliation bill later that night — one clause at most, or nothing); the bishops opposed the final bill; the women religious' letter (NETWORK; sixty-some leaders; Wed Mar 17) and the CHA supported it; **"thirty-two million"** more insured (CBO, that week). **Do not reproduce the shout from the floor** (at most: somebody shouted from the Republican side). The law is **signed Tue Mar 23 — OUT**.
- **St. Joseph customs [R; plan §5.3]:** New Orleans: three tiers; the saint at the top; lilies, candles; breads shaped as his staff, beard, sandals, a hammer, a cross; **fava ("lucky") beans** (the famine legend told as a legend — "they say"); **cuccidati** (fig cookies); **pignolatti** (if the Writer is unsure, omit); lemons (a blessed lemon is lucky — hedge, "Mémère says"); stuffed artichokes; pasta with **"St. Joseph's sawdust"** (toasted breadcrumbs); a fried fish; **meatless**; blessed by a priest; **the tupa tupa** (the Holy Family knocks, is refused twice, admitted the third time; the "saints" fed first); the food given to the poor; **a piece of blessed bread kept against storms** (hedged in Lena's mouth: "Mémère keeps a piece in the kitchen for hurricanes"). Calabrese/Youngstown: *la tavola di San Giuseppe*; **lagane e ceci**; **zeppole di San Giuseppe** (cream, a candied cherry); the children as the saints; the fave. **Youngstown wedding soup** (escarole, tiny meatballs, acini di pepe).
- **Trumbull (s16) [R]:** the published register (S2E10 s07): *"This is a prepaid call from —" "Danny." "— an inmate at Trumbull Correctional Institution. This call is subject to monitoring and recording. To accept, press one."* — paraphrase or abbreviate it this time (don't paste S2E10's exact sentence run; #199); **fifteen minutes; a one-minute warning**; the prepaid account on Jack's number. No Eastern clock. Fallback in change 17.
- ***Rocky* (1976) [V by the film]:** the first image is a painting of Christ over the ring (the Resurrection Athletic Club); the last word is "Adrian." **Theo's line uses only those two facts.** Not "his priest is the only one who knows his name" (unverified — cut).
- ***Ite ad Ioseph*** — Genesis 41:55 (Pharaoh to the hungry Egyptians: "Go to Joseph") — the old Joseph, on a devotional card. Osei explains it in one sentence.
- **Louisiana French (Mémère):** **one line on the page in French** — *Bonne fête au garçon de l'Ohio* — and the rest in Lena's English. **Calabrese (Nonna):** *Auguri, Giacomino* and nothing else in Italian on the phone.
- ***Brideshead* Book One [R]:** Oxford; plovers' eggs; Aloysius the bear; the picnic; the skull with *Et in Arcadia ego*; Sebastian wanting to bury something precious in every place he's been happy (paraphrase). Lord Marchmain abroad (Caleb's "Some dads just leave the building").
- **The Catechism [R]:** **¶31** "converging and convincing arguments" · **¶32** the world (movement, becoming, contingency, order and beauty) + Augustine, *Sermon* 241 (*question the beauty of the earth, question the beauty of the sea…*) + Rom 1:19–20 · **¶33** the human person (openness to truth and beauty, moral goodness, freedom, conscience, longing for the infinite and for happiness); the soul, "the seed of eternity we bear in ourselves, irreducible to the merely material" · **¶34** the world and man contain "neither their first principle nor their final end" · **¶35** the proofs "predispose one to faith" · **¶36** Vatican I: God can be known "with certainty… by the natural light of human reason" · **¶37** the difficulties; people "persuade themselves that what they would not like to be true is false or at least doubtful" · **¶38** hence revelation · **¶39 heading: "How Can We Speak about God?"** **≤1 sentence quoted per round; paraphrase the rest.**
- **Philosophy (paraphrase only):** the contingency argument (Aquinas's third way; a series that holds things up *now*, not a first domino) · the regress / brute-fact objection · beauty as the beholder's evolved wiring · the genealogical debunking of conscience and its self-undermining reply · physicalism ("the oldest special effect") · the genetic fallacy and Lewis's "Bulverism" (Jack may name Lewis) · ¶35's own concession.
- **Never:** quote a real person; a song lyric; anything after Sun Mar 21 evening; a Gloria at the Sunday Mass; "number one" for any chart song; a real Las Olas business; "liturgical living"; "new moon"; "minus tide" unconfirmed; a cigarette lit.

### 2B.6a The duel `cat2` — design (Low Tide, Wed Mar 17, 4:15–5:40 p.m.; opponent Skye; the second Catechism session, ¶31–38, "The Ways of Coming to Know God" + "The Knowledge of God According to the Church") — **§6.4–§6.6 bind except as corrected here; this is their final form**
- **The frame (s07, no `duel` field):** the wetsuit; "the only green I own"; the surfers; the barista's towel; ten minutes of the class; the binder's tab KNOWING; "convincing" in pink. The HUD comes up at s07a.
- **Positions (real on both sides; never a strawman; Jack doesn't always win):** **Skye (outside the faith, from the craft and the body):** beauty is brought, not told (we evolved to love water and a horizon — the beauty is in the wiring, which is why it feels like a message); the regress (if "nobody made him" is allowed, she'll take it for the sea and save a step); the genealogy of conscience (a voice installed by whoever raised you — hers is Debbie's); the soul as performance ("the oldest special effect there is"); wishful thinking cuts both ways (¶37 swings it at unbelievers only); ¶35 concedes the proofs reach a cause, not anyone you'd light a candle for. **Jack (inside):** contingency — not a first domino but what holds the line up *now*; the sea as a letter, not a speaker (Augustine's "confession" of beauty); the debunking argument saws off its own branch; whoever asks whether a line is *true* isn't Debbie; the genetic fallacy (two biographies, one argument still on the table); the door and the key (¶35 and Chapter Two).
- **Moves (four a round; §2B change 3):** **Round 1** Press · Reframe · Concede · Riposte. **Round 2** Press (with the Reframe's point folded in) · Personal · Concede · Riposte. **Round 3** Press · Concede · Sincerity · Riposte.
- **Scoring:** Press / Reframe / Personal → `duel.cat2.rigor +1`, `skye_cat_jack +1` (Personal also `rel.skye.friction +1`, `rel.skye.trust -1`) · Riposte → `duel.cat2.wit +1`, `rel.skye.warmth +1` · Concede → `duel.cat2.heart +1`, `skye_cat_skye +1`, `rel.skye.trust +1` · **round 3 Sincerity** → `duel.cat2.heart +1`, `skye_cat_jack +1`, `rel.skye.trust +1`, `rel.skye.heat +1` (no trait). **`duel.cat2.skye` and `skye_cat_skye` writer-set `+1` each on the s07b scene set** (round 2 is hers on the room's reading whatever he says — Debbie's voice). **Rounds 1 and 3 are open.**

#### s15 · `FRIDAY · 5:30 PM · DELGADO HALL, THE FISHBOWL — A TABLE FOR A SAINT` · ≈2,300 · **chunk 4 ♥ Romance (Jack/Lena) + Theo, Laney, Margot, Kevin, Osei, Gloria, Rudy, Marcus, strangers from the lobby (a Ferrer kid with a skateboard, two RAs, a Newman soprano — uncast)** · music **Louis Prima — “Buona Sera”** (Theo's iPhone in a coffee mug for a speaker; title only) · set `{"st_joseph_altar":true,"rel.lena.trust":"+1","rel.lena.warmth":"+1","rel.lena.friction":"-1","soul.grace":"+1"}` · choice `s2e22_altar` (**key every option; cue every option: “Lena will remember that.”**) · *that's how much I like you* — **plan §5.0–§5.6 bind**
- **Rules (plan §5.0):** it's hers; a feast with everyone, not a date; the faith true and unglossed (nobody explains the Church to the reader — Lena explains things to the floor in character, Margot footnotes, Laney performs, Osei blesses); **no "love," no "girlfriend"**; **no Skye** (not a mention, not a thought); **Jack receives** — awkward first (he didn't want a fuss), then wrecked by a specific thing, then simply glad; **no speech from Jack.** **Nobody says "custom."**
- **[`s2e21_where`]** ✓ at the lobby doors, before he walks in, Lena: **“You’re going to cry. I want you to.”** (the mirror's one use) · else she just opens the door.
- **Staging (plan §5.2):** the glass-walled Fishbowl; the broken foosball table pushed to the wall under a sheet (Laney's sheet cake hiding on it — **not revealed**, s16a); couches shoved back; everyone watching him come through the lobby doors. **The altar against the long wall: three tiers** (Lena to the floor: one for each Person of the Trinity) **of milk crates and a door off its hinges from the basement, under three white tablecloths** (from three dining halls, to be returned — Laney). **At the top, [`s2e17_wed`] ✓ Mémère's holy card** from his headboard ledge (Theo's key — said by nobody; Jack knows the card, and the narration lets him know it), in a borrowed brass frame between two votives — Joseph with the lily, the Child and the carpenter's square, *PRIEZ POUR NOUS* (else a Newman-table Joseph). **White lilies** from the Albertsons — Lena: **“He gets lilies. It’s his whole look.”**
- **What's on it (plan §5.3; §2B.2 hedges; the Writer chooses and specifies, doesn't list everything):** the breads Rudy shaped with her — **the staff** (flowers of dough at the top), **the beard** (braided), **sandals**, **a hammer**, **a cross** — two came out dark (the oven runs hot at the back) and went to the back row — **and one shaped, unmistakably, like the state of Ohio** (Lena's line about it is new — not "Flat. No weather." (S2E21's) — e.g., "It's the shape of a man who's never been told no in his life," or the Writer's); **Nonna's fava beans** in a bowl (the bean that kept people alive in a famine, *they say*); **Celeste's cuccidati** from Dupre Street in a Royal Dansk tin (one clause); **zeppole** (Nonna's way); **lemons and oranges**; **stuffed artichokes**; **pasta with St. Joseph's sawdust** (toasted breadcrumbs — Margot's footnote, one sentence); **the lagane e ceci** (the chickpeas of Wednesday — **he gets it now; one clause, no gloss**); **Rudy's whole fried fish**. **[`s2e19_roux`]** ✓ on the bottom tier, among the lemons, **the unopened Barq's** from Mission Lanes; he knows what it is; she doesn't look at him while he sees it (else nothing). **Meatless, all of it** — Lena, one sentence, to the floor: it was a Lenten table before it was a feast, and you don't change a recipe on a saint. **On a side table, NOT on the altar, a stockpot with its lid on and a ladle beside it** — **not described, not smelled, not explained** until the lid comes off (#297).
- **Osei blesses the altar** (holy water from a jam jar; the old Latin and then English; short). 🔒 **One clause, no gloss:** at "bless all fathers" his voice does one small thing and goes on; **Jack notices only that Osei looks at the card at the top a moment longer than at the bread.** He blesses the fava bowl. (#86: no delight tags.)
- **The tupa tupa (Laney stages it).** Laney tries first to cast Jack and Lena as Joseph and Mary — **Lena: “Absolutely not.”** (flat). **One look between Laney and Theo across the altar — the only one** (the alliance visible, never said; nobody else sees it but Jack). So: **Margot is Joseph** (deadpan, a mop handle for a staff with a lily duct-taped to the top: **“I’ve done the reading.”**); **Laney is Mary** (a blue Delgado shower curtain; pageant posture); **the Bambino is Coco the coconut**; **Theo is the innkeeper** who refuses them twice with loglines (**“No room. There’s a convention. Orthodontists.”** and one more of the Writer's); and **Kevin, who happens to be standing at the door, opens it the third time** — Laney seats him with the saints, **“because the brokenhearted count, sugar,”** (sugar #2) and he lets her. 🔒 Kevin: nothing implied; he is simply fed first. **Not Theo as Joseph with a child in his arms; Evan nowhere.**
- **Then everyone eats, standing, off paper plates**, and **the strangers come in because the doors are glass**: the Ferrer kid with a skateboard; two RAs; **Marcus from the desk** ("It still says Broussard," one line at most, or none); a Newman soprano (the one who told Laney — unremarked). **Gloria and Rudy** at the end of the table (Gloria's pie is under a dish towel — s16a). (#278: one sentence each at most, at the moment each matters; nobody gets a button.)
- **Jack's receiving, in three movements** (narration, not speech): awkward (the BIRTHDAY BOY sash is NOT on yet — s16a); then **wrecked by a specific thing** (the card at the top; or Ohio — the Writer picks one); then the soup.
- **The soup (plan §5.6 — VERBATIM where bold):** when he has seen the altar and can't say anything, Lena brings him to the side table and lifts the lid of the Anchor's stockpot. **His mother's wedding soup** — he knows it by the smell before he looks: escarole, the meatballs the size of marbles, acini di pepe. **Meat. On a Friday. In Lent.** He looks at her. She has never in her life eaten meat on a Friday in Lent; on Dupre Street the altar is meatless and so is the house (she says so, or he knows it). **“It’s a solemnity. The Church says I may.”** — a beat — **“It doesn’t say I have to.”** — she ladles two cups, hands him one, lifts hers: **“That’s how much I like you, Costigan.”** Then she eats a meatball, solemnly, like a woman taking an oath, and makes a face at how good it is. (The joke is on her own habits; the theology is right — §2B.2.)

#### s21 · `SUNDAY · 7:35 PM · DELGADO HALL, THE FISHBOWL — THE VOTE` · ≈1,300 · **chunk 5 ♥ Romance (Jack/Lena — direct opposition as sport) + Margot (for the *Tide*), Theo (a book on the count), Laney, Kevin; Caleb and Yusuf optional** · no music (C-SPAN on the Fishbowl's TV) · set `{"vote_night":true,"nuns_letter":true}` · choice `s2e22_vote` (**key every option**; no cue) · *told you* — **plan §8 binds (as corrected)**
- **The altar's ghost:** the milk crates gone, one lily left in a Big Gulp cup on the TV. **C-SPAN; the count running.** Public record only (§2B.2): Stupak's bloc announced this afternoon they'd vote yes after the President agreed to an executive order on abortion funding; **the House passes the Senate bill, 219 to 212** (said once), ≈7:45; somebody shouted from the Republican side (**not reproduced**). **No real person quoted.**
- **Both furious, differently (no strawmen; each grants the other's best point on some option):** **Lena** — a pro-life Catholic Worker Democrat who wanted the coverage *and* the Stupak language, believed Stupak in November, calls the executive order **“a press release with a seal on it,”** betrayed by her own party and torn between **the sixty-some heads of women's orders who wrote Congress Wednesday** and the bishops — glad for the millions and sick about the price. **Jack** — a Rust Belt law-and-order conservative against the bill on its merits (federal reach, the cost, the funding language), who called it Wednesday: **“Told you.”** (once, in the body, before the choice).
- **The ensemble — one sentence each, at the moment it matters (#278):** **Margot** at the coffee table with a laptop for the *Tide*: **“I’m writing *historic* in brackets until I decide how I feel.”** · **Theo** running a book on the margin out of the Oscar coffee can (apolitical and delighted; he pays out to Yusuf if Yusuf is there) · **Kevin**, the pre-med, quietly for the bill, for the one reason he gives once: **his mother's counter at the Walgreens in San Jose, and the man who put his insulin back** (continuity) — and not a word more · **Laney** furious at everyone on principle, then crying a little at the count for no reason she'll name.
- **Choice `s2e22_vote`** (key every option; no cue; **it ends in affection, not a truce** — each response differently):
  - [Candor] *An executive order can be torn up with a pen. You can’t vote for a law on a promise.* · set `{"s2e22_vote":"candor","trait.candor":"+1"}` · key “When the House passed the health-care bill, you told Lena an executive order could be torn up with a pen, and she said that was exactly why she was angry.” · → s22 · *“I *know*. That’s why I’m *angry*. Not because you’re right.” (No vocative — §2B counts.) She does not kiss him; she throws a couch cushion, accurately. Last beat: on Lena, arms folded, furious, beside him on the couch anyway.*
  - [Concede] “Thirty-two million people get a doctor. I can’t sneer at that.” · set `{"s2e22_vote":"concede","rel.lena.trust":"+1"}` · key “When the House passed the health-care bill, you granted Lena that thirty-two million people getting a doctor was nothing to sneer at.” · → s22 · *Lena, stopped: “No. You can’t.” And she's angrier now, at having to hold both, and kisses him to end a sentence she's losing. Last beat: on her, pulling back, still scowling at C-SPAN.*
  - [Wit] “Your party just cut a deal with a Catholic from Michigan over a piece of paper. Welcome to my world.” · set `{"s2e22_vote":"wit","trait.wit":"+1","rel.lena.warmth":"+1"}` · key “When the House passed the health-care bill, you welcomed Lena to your world, and she reminded you it had Ohio in it.” · → s22 · *“*Your* world has *Ohio* in it.” (Lena's "Youngstown"-adjacent jab; no "Flat. No weather.") She kisses him quick, as a rebuttal. Last beat: Theo, from the coffee can: "Is that a concession?" — Lena: "It's a *point of order*."*
  - [Sincerity] “I hate that the people you voted for did this to you. I’d hate it if it were mine.” · set `{"s2e22_vote":"sincerity","trait.sincerity":"+1","rel.lena.trust":"+1"}` · key “When the House passed the health-care bill, you told Lena you hated that she’d been betrayed by people she voted for.” · → s22 · *She looks at him a long time. “It *will* be yours one day. Remember you said that.” (A seed of the long arc #47 — no prophecy beyond that line.) She puts her head on his shoulder and watches the reconciliation vote with him without a word. Last beat: on her head on his shoulder.*
- **Never:** a real person quoted; the shout reproduced; Lena's party or Jack's mocked; John 8 mentioned at the vote; "it's not nothing"; anything after Sunday evening (the signing is Tuesday — not said).

#### s22 · `SUNDAY · 9:20 PM · DELGADO HALL, HER STEP — SOMETHING NEW` · ≈500 · **chunk 5 ♥ Romance (Jack/Lena)** · no music · set `{"rel.lena.trust":"+1"}` · **`end: true`** (no choice) · *straining forward*
- **After the vote, on her step.** She's still angry; she leans back against his shins anyway. **Frank's text** (final; the third of the week; no Eastern clock):
  - `{"type":"sms","from":"Dad","text":"PASSED. YOUR MOTHER SAYS GOOD. I SAY WE’LL SEE. STUPAK SHOULD HAVE HELD.","time":"9:20 PM"}`
  - Lena reads it over his arm: **“Your father agrees with me.”** — **“My father agrees with my mother.”** — no fight.
- **¶39 open on his knee — “How Can We Speak about God?”** (the next Wednesday's) — and **the Ohio bread in a napkin between them**: she breaks a corner off it — Ashtabula, he thinks — and gives him half, and keeps the rest (the heel stays hers; it can come back at Easter).
- **Once, maybe, stones** (plan §8.3) — she says something about who throws the first one, or doesn't; never a sermon; never aimed.
- **The last beat is hers (#275 — a person, not an object):** she reads ¶39's first line aloud over his shoulder, unasked, in her lector's voice and then in her own — and says **“Wednesday,”** and nothing after it; the word has nothing in it but the day (**never a joke, never a crack** — #80; she has said it as a fact for two weeks). End.
- **Never:** "love"; "Then it's Wednesday."; Skye's name; a closed phone (#264); an object as the last image.

### THE ROMANCE LADDER — FINAL (Voice Editor checks it; ids per §2B; **kissing only — no making out; no "love," no "girlfriend," no "boyfriend," no "official" between Jack and Lena on any path**)
1. **s01** — the gift reversed: *FRIDAY'S MINE.* (his word, her capitals); a quick kiss hello at the Commons table, over a blank legal pad.
2. **s05** — a floury hand flat on his chest; "You didn't see me." — kissing as conspiracy.
3. **s06** — "Tell me after." — she lets him go to Skye without a joke, and asks only to be told.
4. **s08** — **the first hurt since the levee that isn't a fight**: "Thank you for telling me."; "Was she good?"; he names it, or he says it's only Wednesday, or he waits; **she comes back** — the kiss there (or the cheek) is the coming-back, not a reward.
5. **s12** — midnight: the first kiss of nineteen, hers, sugar and anise; she won't stay (dough rising) — the first time she chooses a thing for him over a minute with him.
6. **s14** — white lace; "That was kind of her."; a zeppola at noon on a Friday, watched like a fuse.
7. **s15** — the altar: he receives it; **"That's how much I like you, Costigan."**; in front of everyone, nothing more than her hand on his sleeve (on [Candor]) and his face; the ensemble sees it; Laney's casting refused.
8. **s16** — she speaks to his brother ([Sincerity]) or waits on the step below; she hears "That was always me." (if she's near) and doesn't ask.
9. **s16a** — the bean in his wallet; her hand on it.
10. **s17** — Mémère's words; "I did."; "It was very hard."; **the longest kiss of the week** and **the episode's only "he wants her" paragraph** — the line held, gladly.
11. **s19l** [lena] — wet hands at the dish sink · or **s20** (every path) — the blister on her thumb at the sign of peace.
12. **s21–s22** — opposition as sport, furious at the same Congressman for opposite reasons; one kiss (or a cushion) to end an argument neither has won; her back against his shins; "Wednesday."

### THE SKYE LADDER — FINAL (the whole mix every scene she's in: intellect · wit/play · sex appeal · fun · one dropped voice)
1. **s04** — Fenner follows up; the pilings; "I was wrong about him. I *hate* that."; the A; "Bring the good ones. I read ahead again." (sex appeal: one sentence — her hair wet from the morning's water.)
2. **s07** — out of the water; the only green she owns; "Last week was wardrobe."; the surfers talk swell with her; [gown wit] "Now who's underdressed?"; [c3 reframe] "I turned it over till Saturday… I don't know how you'd play it."
3. **s07a–s07c** — the sea said nothing (the surf hotline); **Debbie's voice — the dropped voice of the session, round 2**; the soul as the oldest special effect; "So where's Chapter Two?"
4. **s07d** — the verdict and the count in her hand; "Bring adjectives."; out the back door to the bike path with the board, barefoot, and doesn't look back.
5. **s14** — her texts: she did not post on his wall, and says so; "31 was better."
6. **Never:** a touch (she never; he never), a lean toward a kiss, anything about Lena, anything about a promise, "I can work with that." **She doesn't know about Friday's altar.**

## 5. THE BIRTHDAY — THE SPEC (s12–s17; Romance Writer, Opus; the Knowledge Auditor, the Standards & Spoiler Guard and the Period Researcher sign §5.3–§5.5)
### 5.0 Rules
1. **It's hers.** Lena planned it, alone in the deciding and with everyone in the doing. Jack is told nothing until he walks into the Fishbowl at 5:30; **the leaks are comic and the player enjoys them** (s01, s05, s08, s10a, s11, s12) — he never pieces together the whole; he is *surprised* (and the narration lets him be, without a paragraph of him being surprised).
2. **Not a date** (brief: dates avoid Lenten Fridays). **A feast with everyone**: the floor, the Newman crowd, Osei, Gloria and Rudy, strangers from the lobby (the custom: an altar is open to anyone who comes) [R].
3. **The faith true and unglossed** (bible §4.5): the solemnity is a real feast; the altar is a real devotion; the blessing is a real blessing; the food really goes to the poor; **nobody explains the Church to the reader** — Lena explains things to the floor in character, Margot footnotes, Laney performs, Osei blesses.
4. **Lena never says "love"; nobody says "girlfriend."** The ensemble's eyes say what nobody says (the alliance's terror of jinxing it; one look between Theo and Laney; Kevin's "Respectfully" if needed).
5. **No Skye at the feast** — not a mention, not a thought in the narration after s14's text (the evening is theirs and everyone's).
6. **Jack receives.** The man who keeps faith with the absent is, for once, the one kept faith with. Write his receiving honestly: awkward first (he didn't want a fuss), then wrecked by a specific thing (the soup; the card at the top; Ohio), then simply glad. **He does not make a speech.**

### 5.1 The secret, Mon–Thu (the leaks; each pays at the altar)
Mon s01 *FRIDAY'S MINE.*; the blank legal pad · Tue s05 the walk-in, Rudy, the flour, the anise; "You didn't see me." · Wed s08 KEEP OUT on the second-floor kitchen door in Laney's pageant capitals, the smell of fig and orange peel and frying through the gap · Thu s10a Nonna's box of fave to MISS LENA BROUSSARD · Thu s11 "I've got bread." · Fri 12:01 a.m. s12 the flour in her hair. *(Off page, said at the altar or on her landing as needed — never all of it: **she called the Glenwood landline Mon Mar 15 at ≈6:30 a.m. Pacific (9:30 Eastern), from the Fishbowl's pay-phone corner, before he came down** — that's why her step was empty; Ma read her the wedding soup and Nonna, through Ma, the lagane e ceci and the zeppole, and Nonna mailed the fave that afternoon; Celeste Express-Mailed four dozen cuccidati in a Royal Dansk tin from Dupre Street; Theo lent his key so she could take Mémère's holy card off 412's headboard ledge Thursday night and put it back Saturday; Rudy and Gloria gave her the Anchor's ovens after close Thursday and the fryer at five Friday morning; Laney did the tablecloths (three, from three dining halls, returned); Margot did the research; Kevin did the knife work.)*

### 5.2 Staging — the Fishbowl at 5:30
The glass-walled ground-floor lounge (§25.5), its broken foosball table pushed to the wall and covered with a sheet, the couches shoved back. **The altar against the long wall**: **three tiers** (the Trinity) built of milk crates and a door off its hinges from the basement, under three white tablecloths; at the top, in a borrowed brass frame with two votives, **Mémère's holy card** (`s2e17_wed=="house"`) — Saint Joseph with the lily, the Child and the carpenter's square, *PRIEZ POUR NOUS* — [else: Mémère's mailed card, opened early by permission]; **white lilies** from the Albertsons (Lena: "He gets lilies. It's his whole look."). Everyone watching him come through the lobby doors.

### 5.3 What's on it (New Orleans and Calabrese — every item [R]; the Writer uses what the Researcher confirms and hedges the rest)
- **The breads** (Rudy shaped them with her; the Anchor's oven runs hot at the back — two came out dark and she put them at the back): **St. Joseph's staff** (a long loaf with "flowers" of dough at the top), **his beard** (braided), **his sandals**, **a hammer**, **a cross** — **and one shaped, unmistakably, like the state of Ohio** ("It's the shape of a man who's never been told no in his life," Lena says, of Ohio. — or the Writer's line. "Flat. No weather." is spent; don't reuse).
- **Fava ("lucky") beans** in a bowl — **Nonna's**, from the box (the custom on both sides of the family: Sicilian New Orleans and Calabrese Youngstown — the bean that kept people alive in a famine [R: hedge as legend]).
- **Cuccidati** (fig cookies) — **Celeste's**, from Dupre Street; **pignolatti** if the Researcher confirms a New Orleans altar standard [R]; **zeppole di San Giuseppe** (Calabrese — Nonna's way, cream and a candied cherry) [R]; **lemons and oranges** (a blessed lemon is lucky in New Orleans [R — hedge]).
- **Stuffed artichokes** (the New Orleans altar's) · **pasta with "St. Joseph's sawdust"** (toasted breadcrumbs for the sawdust of his shop) [R] · **lagane e ceci** (wide noodles with chickpeas — Calabria's St. Joseph dish; Nonna's recipe through two phones) [R] · **a whole fried fish** (Rudy's; the Anchor's) · a frittata.
- **Meatless, all of it — the New Orleans custom of the altar, kept even on a solemnity** (Lena says why in one sentence: it was a Lenten table before it was a feast, and you don't change a recipe on a saint).
- **[`s2e19_roux=="sincerity"`, optional, one clause]** on the bottom tier, unopened, **the Barq's** from Mission Lanes — for fathers' hands. Unexplained to anyone; he knows what it is; she doesn't look at him while she sets it there.
- **On a side table, NOT on the altar: Ma's wedding soup** (escarole, the tiny meatballs the size of marbles, acini di pepe; Nonna's meatballs) in the Anchor's stockpot, with a ladle (§5.6).
- **Laney's Texas sheet cake**, exiled by Lena to the foosball table under the sheet ("It's *cake*, Laney, not a sacramental."), with nineteen candles, sung over at 7:40 (s16a).

### 5.4 The blessing and the tupa tupa
- **Osei blesses the altar** (holy water from a jam jar, the old Latin and then English, short), and — 🔒 **one clause, no gloss** — at "bless all fathers" his voice does one small thing and goes on (Kwame; never said; Jack notices only that Osei looked at the card at the top for a moment longer than at the bread). He blesses the fava bowl.
- **The tupa tupa** (Sicilian New Orleans: the Holy Family knocks three times at a door, is refused twice, and admitted the third; they are seated and served first — "the saints") [R]. **Laney stages it.** Laney tries first to cast **Jack and Lena as Joseph and Mary** — **Lena: "Absolutely not."** (flat; a look between Laney and Theo across the altar, the only one — the alliance visible, never said). So: **Margot is Joseph** (deadpan, a mop handle for a staff with a lily duct-taped to the top: "I've done the reading."), **Laney is Mary** (in a blue Delgado shower curtain, pageant posture), **the Bambino is Coco the coconut** (canon), **Theo is the innkeeper** who refuses them twice with loglines ("No room. There's a convention. Orthodontists."), and **Kevin, who happens to be standing at the door, opens it the third time** — and Laney seats him with the saints, "because the brokenhearted count, sugar," and he lets her. (🔒 Kevin: nothing implied; he's simply fed first.) **Not Theo as Joseph with a child in his arms; Evan nowhere.**
- Then everyone eats, standing, off paper plates (Danny calls at 6:15 — s16 — and Jack steps out), and the strangers from the lobby come in because the doors are glass: a kid from Ferrer with a skateboard, two RAs, Marcus from the desk, a Newman soprano — the custom honored without anyone saying "custom."

### 5.5 Danny (s16) — the brother (🔒 the elision rule binds; the Standards & Spoiler Guard signs every line)
- **The call:** **6:15 p.m. Pacific (9:15 at Trumbull)**, in the middle of the feast — [R] the evening phone schedule; if 9:15 p.m. Eastern is past phone hours, move it to **4:45 p.m. Pacific** (before the altar: Jack on his step getting ready, Lena running up to fetch him and arriving for the last half — [Sincerity] still works). The recorded announcement (an inmate at Trumbull Correctional Institution; this call is subject to monitoring; to accept…) [R: paraphrase]; the prepaid account on Jack's number (canon S1E03). Jack takes it on the east stairwell, the Fishbowl's noise and Louis Prima coming up through the concrete.
- **Danny's register** (bible §10.8): funny, profane in a clean way, "Professor," a joke and a cough; one sentence that stops Jack cold. **He knows from Ma** (her Wednesday call with him, off-page) that "the veil girl's doing the table." He sings one bar of *Happy Birthday* badly to make Jack laugh. He asks: **"Who played Joseph?"** — "Margot." — "Who's Margot?" — "You'd like her. She has footnotes." — the one big Danny laugh of the call; then, still laughing, lighter than anything: **"That was always me. You were the baby."** — and goes straight on to something else (the C.O.'s *Brideshead* — "he still says it's about a house"; Reggie; the Cavs). 🔒 **Never glossed. The narration declines the memory** — Jack sits with the phone, and the line goes past him the way a car goes past a man on a curb; the next sentence is about something else. (The player will hear that line again, at the wall, S2E30.)
- **"You smoking?"** — "Lent." — "Good." (the pack's paragraph can live here instead of with Theo — one place only).
- **The choice `s2e22_danny`** (key every option; cue on [Sincerity] only):
  - **[Sincerity]** *Hand Lena the phone.* (she has followed him out to the stairwell with two paper plates; `danny_met_lena`) — she tells Danny what was on the table, all of it, the way she'd tell a parish; Danny goes quiet at the zeppole ("Nonna's?"); he says, to her, **"Thanks for doing it. Somebody ought to."** (S2E06/E08's register: "Somebody in this family ought to be."). She gives the phone back without a word and sits on the step below. · `rel.lena.trust +1`, cue **"Danny will remember that."** · Key: *"When Danny called from Trumbull on your birthday, you handed Lena the phone so she could tell him about the table."*
  - **[Candor]** "She did the whole thing, D. Nonna's table. In a dorm." — Danny: "*Good.*" (and a long breath on the line, the closest Danny comes) · `soul.honesty +1` · Key: *"…you told him Lena had done Nonna's whole table, in a dorm."*
  - **[Restraint]** *Keep him to yourself for the fifteen minutes.* — they talk about nothing — the Cavs, Reggie, the weather in Leavittsburg — till the one-minute warning, and it's the best fifteen minutes of the day; Lena waits at the foot of the flight and doesn't come up · `trait.control +1` · Key: *"…you kept him to yourself for all fifteen minutes."*
- The one-minute warning; "Love you, Professor. Happy birthday." (**Danny may say "love you"** — he's his brother; it's Jack and Lena who don't.) The line goes dead the way prison lines do. **Ends on a person** (Lena on the step below, or Jack), not on the phone.

### 5.6 "That's how much I like you, Costigan." (s15 — VERBATIM, every path)
After the blessing and the tupa tupa, when he has seen the altar and can't say anything: Lena brings him to the side table and lifts the lid of the Anchor's stockpot. **His mother's wedding soup** — he knows it by the smell before he looks: the escarole, the meatballs the size of marbles. **Meat. On a Friday. In Lent.** He looks at her. She has never in her life eaten meat on a Friday in Lent; on Dupre Street the altar is meatless and so is the house (she says so, or he knows it). **"It's a solemnity. The Church says I may."** — a beat — **"It doesn't say I have to."** — and she ladles two cups, and hands him one, and lifts hers. **"That's how much I like you, Costigan."** Then she eats a meatball, solemnly, like a woman taking an oath, and makes a face at how good it is. (Never a joke at the faith: the joke is on her own habits; the theology is right — §0.) **The choice `s2e22_altar`** (key every option; **cue every option: "Lena will remember that."**) — what he says when he can say something:
- **[Sincerity]** "It tastes like Glenwood." — she: "Your mother said you'd say that. She said if you didn't, I'd used the wrong escarole." · `rel.lena.warmth +1` · Key: *"At the St. Joseph altar Lena built for your birthday, you tasted your mother's wedding soup and told her it tasted like home."*
- **[Candor]** "Nobody's done this since my brother went away." — (it's true; it's not a repeat of Sunday — he's telling her what it *means* now that it's done) — she: "I know. That's why I did it." · `rel.lena.trust +1` · Key: *"…you told her nobody had done this since your brother went away."*
- **[Wit]** "You ate meat on a Friday. For me. I'm telling Mémère." — "Mémère *told* me to." · `rel.lena.warmth +1` · Key: *"…you threatened to tell her grandmother she'd eaten meat on a Friday."*
- *(optional fourth, the Writer's — on [`s2e21_where=="candor"`] only, one use of the mirror: before he walks in, she: "You're going to cry. I want you to." — this lives in the scene's body, not as an option.)*
**The gifts** (after; each one line, each in character, none a button — tic #278): **Kevin** — a box of medical-grade nitrile gloves, size L, for the Hobart: "For the maceration." (`s2e21_kevin` paid, every path — it was on the page for all) · **Theo** — a framed one-sheet of *Rocky* (1976) — "The most Catholic film ever made, Costigan. He blesses himself before every fight and his priest is the only one who knows his name." [R: the Rocky details — Rocky blesses himself; the priest at the start? hedge] · **Margot** — a typed index card: *FIELD NOTE. St. Joseph is patron of workers, fathers, the dying, the Universal Church, and people trying to sell a house. You are at least two of these.* · **Laney** — a pageant sash, *BIRTHDAY BOY*, rhinestones, worn over the Carhartt for the cake · **Gloria** — a coconut cream pie, "Don't argue with me," and Rudy's handshake · **Osei** — a holy card, *Ite ad Ioseph* ("Go to Joseph" — Genesis 41:55, the old Joseph; Osei explains it in one sentence with a laugh). **Lena's own gift is the altar, the soup and — later, on her landing — Mémère's words.** (No wrapped present from Lena.)

### 5.7 Ma at 9:41 Eastern (s13)
Ma has called at the minute every year of his life — **9:41 Eastern, wherever he was; last year at Mooney, in the hall outside Latin** (new canon, harmless). **Nothing about any earlier March 19 beyond the table and the laundry basket** (the elision rule; never invent a memory near Danny's charge). She says the weight (seven pounds, eleven ounces [new canon]) because Frank makes her. **"Lena called me Monday."** (her route: Ma tells him plainly; she isn't keeping Lena's secret about *calling*, only about what for — "I'm not telling you what about. She'd kill me. I like her.") Nonna on the extension in Calabrese — **"*Auguri, Giacomino.*"** and something about the fave ("She says the girl has the beans. She says that's all she'll say on a telephone.") [R: Calabrese phrasing; keep to Italian a reader can follow]. Then Ma, lighter than she means it: **"When you were little Nonna did the table in the dining room. Danny was always Joseph. You were too little to be anything but the Bambino. You used to cry when they put you in the laundry basket."** — 🔒 the line Danny will echo at 6:15 (Ma's version is a memory; Danny's is something else; never glossed; the narration does not dwell on either). `s2e22_ma` (no key): [Sincerity] "Thanks for calling at the minute, Ma." / [Wit] "Seven pounds eleven. I've been carrying it around." / [Candor] "I didn't think anybody would do the table again." — Ma: "Neither did I." (on candor; one beat; then "Eat something.") **Frank** (text, ALL CAPS): *HAPPY BIRTHDAY. 19. YOUR MOTHER SAYS I HAVE TO SAY THE WEIGHT. 7 LBS 11 OZ.* · **Gina** (`s2e19_status=="candor"`): *happy bday old man* / *19 is still a teenager. dont get excited* — else Gina by text without the status reference. (Rosie's drawing comes by mail, s16a.)

### 5.8 Her landing at 11:48 (s17) — Mémère's words, who told her, "It was very hard."
- Twelve minutes left of the nineteenth. Her landing; the stairwell window; her socks (not the white ones — she has those in her drawer; one clause). **Mémère's mailed card** out of her jean jacket. She reads him the line she read at the altar, and then the rest of the card, in Cajun French and then in English [R: the Researcher renders it in Louisiana French — not Paris French, Mémère would like that understood]. Then, without the card: **what Mémère said on Ash Wednesday, over the holy card, that Lena translated as only "She says Joseph was a good man."** — the rest of it now (the Writer finds Mémère's words; their substance, binding): **Joseph never says one word in the whole Gospel, and God trusted him with everything — the girl and the child; a man who can wait is worth ten who can talk; and *that one* — the boy from Ohio — waits like a man who's had practice.** Lena: "I didn't translate that part. I was holding a sweater." (S2E17 s05h: she was folding a sweater — "She shakes the sweater out and folds it again." ✓)
- **"Who told her?"** — he asks it, finally (knowledge.md: unresolved since S2E17). **"I did."** — **When:** **at Christmas, on the phone to Breaux Bridge, the week she was deciding about Julian** (consistent with the Mardi Gras honesty, item 6: she meant to end it after the break, for Jack) — "She asked me who I was thinking about so hard I'd let the roux burn, and I told her your birthday instead of your name. It was the only thing about you I could say out loud." — **no "love"; no Julian by name if she can help it** (once, plainly, if she must).
- **The mirror** (`s2e21_hour=="sincerity"` ✓ — "It wasn't hard." / "That's what I mean."): **"I'll have you know this was very hard."** — "The soup?" — "*All* of it. Your grandmother writes in a code. Rudy's oven is a liar. Laney wanted a *fog machine*." [else-branches: on `=="candor"` "I thought about the soup since Monday." (his socks line returned); on `=="wit"` "It cost a great deal more than five dollars."; on `=="restraint"` she says nothing about it and kisses him first.]
- **The kiss** — the longest of the week, at 11:58; the one "he wants her" paragraph of the episode (tic #274; frank, literary, the order he feels in it — *her first*; and the line held, gladly; **no making out**: hands in hair, a hand at her waist over the sweater, her fist in the Carhartt again — not the same gesture as E21 s15c (tic #298): this time **both her hands flat on his chest, the way she stopped him by the Hobart, and then not stopping him**, and she ends it at 12:00 because "It's not your birthday anymore. I don't kiss commoners." — or the Writer's).
- **The choice `s2e22_twelve`** (key every option; **cue every option: "Lena will remember this."**):
  - **[Sincerity]** "Best birthday I've had since I was a kid." — (#289 "in my life" superlative budget — this one is scoped, allowed once) · Key: *"When your birthday ended on her landing, you told Lena it was the best one you'd had since you were a kid."*
  - **[Candor]** "You told her my birthday at Christmas." — "I did." — "I was with —" — "I know who you were with. I told her anyway." · Key: *"…you made her say again that she'd told her grandmother your birthday at Christmas."*
  - **[Restraint]** *Say nothing. Let it be midnight.* · Key: *"…you let it turn midnight without saying anything."*
- **Ends on her** — going down the half flight to two in her socks, turning at the wired glass: one line, or none (the Writer's; not a button on an object).

### 5.9 Skye's text (s14, 9:12 a.m.) — and Lena's "That was kind of her."
- **Skye knows his birthday** (Facebook's birthday reminder — period-accurate [V-ish, R]; and December). **She does not post on his wall** — she knows how his wall reads to Glenwood and the Newman crowd (the singalong lesson, canon). **Text, lowercase, no apostrophes, her register:** e.g., *hi. happy birthday costigan* / *i didnt post on your wall. youre welcome* / *19 is a good number. 31 was better* (¶31 — the section; the joke is hers and is about the book). **Nothing about Lena; nothing about a promise; nothing knowing.**
- He shows it to Lena unasked in the courtyard after Mass (`skye_text_shown` precedent). **"That was kind of her."** — level; true; she means it and it costs her; she hands the phone back and turns to the zeppola. **Never a joke.** (The Knowledge Auditor: Lena knows Skye texts him; she now knows Skye knows his birthday.)
- His reply to Skye (no choice; one line of narration): he texts *thank you* and nothing else, before Mass ("Bring adjectives" is hers, next Wednesday; he doesn't have it yet). The Architect may make it a no-key choice — *thank you* / *thanks. 31 was better* / no reply — gating nothing on it.

### 5.10 Length and register
s12 ≈800 · s13 ≈850 · s14 ≈1,050 · s15 ≈2,400 · s16 ≈950 · s16a ≈550 · s17 ≈1,100 → ≈7,700 across Thu midnight–Fri (the Friday act ≈6,900). **Warm, funny, specific; the happiest Jack has been on the page since the parade; one ache (Danny), unglossed.** No music but Louis Prima.
