## 6. SESSION TWO — THE SPEC (s04 the set-up; s07–s07d the session and duel `cat2`; Romance Writer, Opus; the Voice Editor and the Knowledge Auditor sign it)
### 6.0 Rules
1. **Skye is magnificent, funny, brilliant and unbeaten in spirit** — and this week she's *ahead*: she lost session one and has been reading for a week. The player should want next Wednesday again.
2. **The whole mix** (brief, standing): intellect (the duel at full strength), wit/play (the sea report, the surfers, the drama-teacher warm-up), sex appeal (the wetsuit — frank, literary, described once), fun (she surfed two hours for a paragraph) — **and one dropped voice per session** (round 2, this time — §6.4).
3. **She never touches him; he never touches her.** Not the towel (she throws the barista's second towel over her own shoulder, never toward him), not the coffee (set down), not a hand on the binder they share. **The narration does NOT announce "She never touched him" again** (it did, once, in E21; tic #280) — the staging shows it.
4. **She does not know Lena's condition exists.** No line of hers can be read as reacting to it; no line of Jack's in her hearing mentions a promise or terms; she does not lean toward a kiss; the narration of her scenes does not wink at it. **"I can work with that" is not said by anyone.**
5. **Never a strawman; Jack doesn't always win.** Her arguments are real philosophy (§6.4) made in an actor's and a surfer's terms. **Round 2 is hers by writer-set point; she takes the session on ≈35% of combinations** (§6.5).
6. **Never cruel; never at Lena's expense; never "Ted"/Theo; never "I knew."** She doesn't ask about Lena, doesn't name her, doesn't learn of the altar or the dates.
7. **Distinct register from Jack–Lena duels** (Lena argues from inside the tradition; Skye from outside it and from the craft and the body — *what does the character want; is the line true or a line; what does it feel like from the yard*). **Vary the round shapes** (tic #293): round 1 opens plain and lands on a bit; round 2 opens on a bit and drops into the session's one plain voice; round 3 opens mid-argument, no bit at all, and ends on a voice.
8. **Showbiz register in her mouth only** (#224, #269); **no courtroom words in her mouth** (#11 — "evidence," "verdict," "Exhibit A" are Lena's); her pen/highlighter as a duel-response button ≤2 a session (#292).

### 6.1 The set-up (s04, Tue) — beats
Fenner follows up; the pilings at the minus tide; "I was wrong about him. I *hate* that."; the A; "Did you bring proofs?" — "Tomorrow." — "Bring the good ones. I read ahead again." (§2 s04). Her hair wet from the morning's water — plant.

### 6.2 Low Tide and the wetsuit (s07)
- **Low Tide** (canon S2E21): board racks still up the walls, plywood counter, the menu on a longboard, DOLORES on the espresso machine; the barista with the nose ring; the two surfers in hoodies under the racks. **4:00, Wednesday, St. Patrick's Day**; Las Olas loud with green two blocks over; the shop nearly empty.
- **She's already there**, and wet. **Her board is in the old wall rack** — "the first board in that rack in ten years" (the barista) — a battered single-fin (the Writer's; one clause). **The wetsuit** (described once, literary, never an inventory): **black neoprene peeled to the waist, the arms tied round her hips; a green bikini top, plainly a swimsuit and plainly not for a coffee shop; salt drying white on her shoulders and in the long sun-streaked hair, which is down and tangled and dripping on the binder; sand on her ankles and on the concrete under her chair; a towel round her neck; barefoot; a surfer's shoulders; the freckles across her nose.** It is the girl he went to the Point break with at dawn (bible §10.2, her dares — one clause of memory, no more), and that is exactly the danger: **it isn't a costume.**
- **The comedy:** the barista brings a second towel without being asked and drops it on Skye's chair; **the two surfers talk swell with her as a peer** ("Sands?" — "Waist-high. Glassy till eleven. Then the wind." — and they nod, deeply respectful, and go back to their argument) — *the opposite of last week*; the kid with the skateboard doesn't come in at all (no repeat). **She says what it is, differently from last week** (no repeat of "It isn't a subtle strategy"): **"It's the only green I own. It's St. Patrick's. I'm observant."** — and then plain: **"Last week was wardrobe. This is just what I look like at four o'clock if nobody makes me change."**
- **The section's joke, hers:** ¶32 quotes Augustine — *question the beauty of the sea* [R: exact wording]. **"So I did. For two hours. I'm reporting back."**
- **[`s2e21_gown=="wit"`]** (his "You're overdressed for the desire for God." — "You're underdressed."): **"You told me I was overdressed. I listened. Now who's underdressed?"** — he's in the Carhartt; she's in a bikini top; it lands both ways. [else: nothing.]
- **[`s2e21_c3=="reframe"`]** (the line that wants something back): before the book, plainly, one paragraph — **she turned it over till Friday, like she said, and then Saturday: "A God who doesn't need anything from me. I've never been in a room with anybody like that. I don't know how you'd play it."** — and drops it, and opens the binder. [`=="press"`: Augustine's thirteen books — she bought the *Confessions* and has read two. `=="concede"`: "You said you couldn't prove it, only believe it. I wrote that down. I want to see if it holds." `=="personal"`: she doesn't mention it — she banked it. `=="riposte"`: page eleven was page twelve in her edition, and she's let it go.]
- **[`s2e21_c1=="concede"`]** the binder's ¶27 page: START LOOKING in its box, and under it, new, in green: *still looking.* (One glance; no comment.)
- **Ten minutes of the class first** — the pilings write-up; the A; she's businesslike about the grade.
- **Jack, honestly:** he looks; his body notices — one plain sentence, frank (not a paragraph; never "he wants her" — tic #274 is Lena's); then, one sentence more, the true thing the save gives him (`s2e21_osei=="candor"`: "I still love her. That didn't stop when I left."): **he knows this one better than the gown, and that's worse** — no more than that, no comparison to Lena, never "love" in the narration about either. Then the page. `resolve.jack −1` on scene entry.
- **`s2e22_suit`** (no key, no cue): **[Wit]** "You're dripping on the desire for God." — "The desire for God was *last* week. Keep up." · **[Sincerity]** "How was it out there?" — "Perfect. That's my problem." (she means the argument) · **[Restraint]** *Look at the page.* — "Coward," pleasantly, and she turns to ¶31.

### 6.3 The binder, this week
The tab KNOWING (S2E21's tabs: DESIRE · KNOWING · SPEAKING · REVELATION). Her highlighters (yellow agree, pink don't, green a good line). **¶31's "converging and convincing arguments" in yellow, and "proofs" in pink with a question mark** — "In my business 'convincing' is a note you give an actor who isn't." The receipt in the gutter at ¶31.

### 6.4 The duel `cat2` — DESIGN (three rounds + verdict; opponent Skye; real philosophy on both sides; round 2 hers by writer-set point)
**Round 1 — s07a, ¶31–32 (the ways from the world: motion, becoming, contingency, the world's order and beauty; Augustine's *question the beauty of the sea*; Rom 1:19–20).** *Shape: opens plain, lands on a bit.* *Her move:* the sea report, sincere: she sat past the break for two hours and asked. **"It was beautiful every single time, and it didn't say a word. It wasn't saying anything. I was."** — **(a) beauty is brought, not told** (we're animals who evolved to love water and a horizon; the beauty is in the beholder's wiring, which is exactly why it feels like a message); then the bit — she reads ¶32's list as a **surf-forecast hotline** in a stoned recorded voice (*"Motion: steady. Becoming: waist to chest. Contingency: high. Order and beauty: glassy all morning, blown out by noon."*) — and **(b) the regress / the brute fact**: "And if everything needs a reason, so does yours. Who made him? Don't say *nobody*. If *nobody* is allowed, I'll take it for the sea and save us a step." *(No writer-set point: round 1 is open.)* *Jack's moves* (`s2e22_c1`; no key, no cue):
- **[Press]** *Contingency — not a first domino, what holds the line up now.* — "Everything you sat on for two hours might not have been. The wave, the board, you. Things that might not be don't explain why they are. Something has to be that can't not be, or there'd be nothing at all. That isn't who started it. It's who's holding it up this afternoon." (Aquinas's third way, a per se series — paraphrase) · `duel.cat2.rigor +1`, `skye_cat_jack +1`.
- **[Reframe]** *The sea isn't the speaker. It's the letter.* — "Augustine never says the sea talks. He says its beauty is a confession — somebody else's. You don't ask a letter what it thinks. You ask who wrote it." · `duel.cat2.rigor +1`, `skye_cat_jack +1`.
- **[Concede]** "You're right that *nobody* is allowed. The book doesn't say you can't stop at the sea. It says you won't — that you'll keep asking why there's a sea. If you can stop asking, you win." (¶34's "neither their first principle nor their final end" — true to the text) · `duel.cat2.heart +1`, `skye_cat_skye +1`, `rel.skye.trust +1`.
- **[Riposte]** "Two hours on a board to test one paragraph. Augustine would've hired you." · `duel.cat2.wit +1`, `rel.skye.warmth +1`.
**Round 2 — s07b, ¶33–34 (the ways from the human person: openness to truth and beauty, the sense of moral goodness, freedom and the voice of conscience, the longing for the infinite and for happiness; the soul, "the seed of eternity… irreducible to the merely material").** *Shape: opens on a bit, drops into the session's one plain voice.* *Her move:* the list read as **a drama teacher's warm-up** (a woman in a caftan at a Studio City acting class — "*Openness!* From the diaphragm! *Conscience* — where does it live? *Show* me!"). Then **the dropped voice** — short, flat, no music: **"I know whose voice my conscience has. It's my mother's. It says *again*, and *smile*, and *they're not paying you to have a face like that.* I've had Debbie's voice in my head since I was eleven. You'd call it God's. I know exactly which lot she parks in."** — the **genealogy of conscience** (a voice installed by whoever raised you; the debunking argument — paraphrase, no names needed); and **(b) the soul as performance** (physicalism in an actor's terms): "I can make a whole theater believe there's somebody in here. It's my job. A soul's the oldest special effect there is." *(Writer-set: `duel.cat2.skye +1`, `skye_cat_skye +1` — on scene entry.)* *Jack's moves* (`s2e22_c2`; no key; **cue on [Personal] only: "Skye will remember that."**):
- **[Press]** *The debunking saws off its own branch.* — "If your conscience is only Debbie, so is the voice telling you it's only Debbie. You don't get to trust the one that tells you not to trust it. Some of it's her. Not the part that knows which part." · `duel.cat2.rigor +1`, `skye_cat_jack +1`.
- **[Reframe]** *Debbie says "again." Something else in you says "that's a line."* — "Last week you asked me whether a line was true or just a line. Debbie never asked you that in your life. She wanted it to land. Whoever's asking whether it's *true* — that isn't your mother." · `duel.cat2.rigor +1`, `skye_cat_jack +1`.
- **[Personal]** *"You cried in a parking garage the day you got everything you wanted. That's the longing for the infinite. You told me last Wednesday."* (true — S2E21 s07c, said at this table) · `duel.cat2.rigor +1`, `skye_cat_jack +1`, `rel.skye.friction +1`, `rel.skye.trust −1` · cue **"Skye will remember that."** — her response: she looks at the window; then, level: "I told you that as a *story*." — "I know." — "Okay." She writes something in pink. (Not "That's going in the binder" — S2E21's.)
- **[Concede]** "You're right that some of it's her. Mine's got Ma in it, and Nonna, and a nun with a ruler named Sister Agnes. The book doesn't say conscience comes to you clean. It says it comes." · `duel.cat2.heart +1`, `skye_cat_skye +1`, `rel.skye.trust +1`.
- **[Riposte]** "If your conscience is your mother, mine's mine — and mine would eat yours." — "She *would*. I've heard her on the phone." · `duel.cat2.wit +1`, `rel.skye.warmth +1`. *(Knowledge: Skye has heard Ma on the phone — the January calls; the Knowledge Auditor confirms or the Writer drops "I've heard her on the phone.")*
**Round 3 — s07c, ¶35–38 (the proofs "predispose to faith" but intimacy with God needs revelation, ¶35; Vatican I: God can be known with certainty by the natural light of reason, ¶36; the difficulties — the senses, the imagination, disordered desires, and that people "persuade themselves that what they would not like to be true is false or at least doubtful," ¶37; hence the need of revelation, ¶38).** *Shape: opens mid-argument, no bit; ends on a voice.* *Her move:* she's already turning the book round to him. **(a) Wishful thinking cuts both ways:** "¶37. People who don't believe are people who don't *want* to. That's last week's move with footnotes. Fine. Then you're a person who *does* want to. It cuts exactly as deep both ways, and your book only swings it in one direction." **(b) ¶35 concedes the proofs don't get there:** "And this one admits it. The proofs get you to a *first cause*. Nobody ever lit a candle for a first cause. You need him to show up and *say* something." Then, at the end, the voice — **a nineteenth-century council father** pronouncing ¶36 in a Latin-accented English, *"with certainty, by the natural light of human reason"* — and dropping it on the last word: **"So where's Chapter Two?"** *(No writer-set point: round 3 is open.)* **The pack paragraph is NOT here this week** (it's Friday night). *Jack's moves* (`s2e22_c3`; **key every option**; **cue on [Sincerity] only: "Skye will remember that."**):
- **[Press]** *If wanting cuts both ways, it can't decide anything.* — "You're right, it cuts both ways. So it can't settle anything. If I believe because I want to and you don't because you don't, that's two biographies. The argument's still on the table where we left it, and somebody has to answer it." (the genetic fallacy; Lewis's "Bulverism" — paraphrase, Jack may name Lewis) · `duel.cat2.rigor +1`, `skye_cat_jack +1` · Key: *"When Skye said wanting it to be true cut both ways, you told her that was exactly why it couldn't settle anything — the argument was still on the table."*
- **[Concede]** "The proofs get you to the door. They don't open it. The book says so itself — that's why there's a Chapter Two." · `duel.cat2.heart +1`, `skye_cat_skye +1`, `rel.skye.trust +1` · Key: *"…you granted that the proofs only get you to the door, and that the book says so itself."*
- **[Sincerity]** *Tell her the truth about wanting.* — **"If I believed what I wanted, Skye, I'd believe a lot less of it."** (He doesn't elaborate; neither does the narration. She knows what one of the hard sayings cost her; the line is about the faith's demands, all of them — **not about Lena, not a move, no "love"**; the Voice Editor and the Knowledge Auditor sign it.) — her response: no voice; a long look; "That's the first thing you've said that I believe completely." — and she doesn't write it down. · `duel.cat2.heart +1`, `skye_cat_jack +1`, `rel.skye.trust +1`, `rel.skye.heat +1` · cue **"Skye will remember that."** · Key: *"…you told her that if you believed what you wanted, you'd believe a lot less of it."*
- **[Riposte]** "You want Chapter Two. That's the most promising thing anybody's said at this table." — "Don't get *excited*." · `duel.cat2.wit +1`, `rel.skye.warmth +1` · Key: *"…you told her that wanting Chapter Two was the most promising thing anyone had said at that table."*

### 6.5 The verdict (s07d) — by score, **exhaustive, never nested** (design of record; her point is 1 from round 2 on every path)
- **`duel.cat2.heart>=2`** → **a draw** — a new line, not last week's: **"Draw. The director's awake this time, and he still couldn't pick."** — she writes DRAW in pink inside the back cover.
- **`duel.cat2.heart<=1 && duel.cat2.rigor>=2`** → **his**: **"Fine. Two and three to you."** (the Roman numerals II and III) + one honest note in her own voice about his best move, gated on his round-1/2/3 tags (contingency — "the thing holding it up this afternoon"; Debbie and the line; the two biographies).
- **`duel.cat2.heart<=1 && duel.cat2.rigor<=1`** → **hers**: **"Mine. And I didn't even need the wetsuit."** (not last week's "Write it down.") — she writes the score inside the back cover in green.
- **The running count, gated on `cat1` (exhaustive):** after the verdict line she writes the total inside the back cover, and the Writer reads it: cat1 Jack's (✓ this save) + cat2 Jack's → *COSTIGAN 2 — SKYE 0* ("Don't." she says, before he can) · cat1 Jack's + cat2 hers → *1 — 1* ("Now it's a *show*.") · cat1 Jack's + cat2 draw → *1 — 0 — 1 DRAW* · (and the cat1-draw and cat1-hers rows, for safety). **The counters themselves (`skye_cat_*`) are never shown or named** — this is her handwriting, not the meter.
- **[`s2e22_c2=="personal"`]** an added line on any verdict: **"The parking garage was a cheap shot. It worked. I hate that it worked."** (a new banking line; she keeps it for later).
- Then, every path: **she moves the receipt to ¶39 — "How Can We Speak about God?" — "Next Wednesday: how you can say anything about him at all, if he's that far off. Bring adjectives."** (the S2E23 nextTime). **She goes out the back** — Low Tide's back door onto the bike path — **with the board under her arm, barefoot, the towel over her shoulder, and doesn't look back** (no heels, no doorframe: a different exit). The barista: "Same time next week?" — to Jack. He says yes. (No "Is that *somebody*?" — spent.)

### 6.8 THE CONDITION'S REVEAL — HELD, and the room's recommendation (registry #93)
- **Not in S2E22.** Two meetings is not "several." The player's preference ("prefer holding the reveal scene to a later episode") is honored.
- **The room's recommendation for the planner who places it:** **after Easter — session 5 or later (Wed Apr 7 at the earliest; S2E25–S2E27)**. Why: on **Easter morning (Sun Apr 4 — Skye's twentieth birthday)** Lena's Facebook comes back with *in a relationship with Jack Costigan* (the Vigil, S2E24); Jack texts Skye happy birthday the same day (brief). Skye's next Wednesday is the first after she has seen it. **That is where her strategy changes — where "never touches him" can break — and where the charged moment right before a kiss belongs.** The scene, **verbatim, when it comes** (brief "After S2E20" CORRECTION; bible #93): Jack, quietly: *"Don't. If you do that, I have to stop meeting you for this. I promised."* — Skye: *"You promised?"* — **he quotes Lena's words to him exactly: "If she ever makes a move, it stops. The coffees, the book, all of it."** — Skye: *"So I'm the one not allowed to make a move?"* — Jack: *"You know that's not what she meant."* — **Skye, with the ghost of a grin and a very familiar expression on her face:** *"I can work with that."* (echo of S1E20; `jack_right_now`).
- **Until then:** nothing on the page lets Skye know the condition exists; Osei knows it (osei path), Theo doesn't; Lena never restates it differently.

## 7. WEDNESDAY'S TELLING — THE SPEC (s08, 6:20 p.m., the second-floor kitchen door; Romance Writer)
### 7.1 Rules
1. **He tells her the same day, plainly** (their rule since the levee; `s2e21_promise=="sincerity"` — "You'd have heard about the book before anybody."). **[`osei_cat`] he tells the funny part first** (Osei's advice, E21 s08o): the surfers talking swell with her, the barista's second towel, "the only green I own." Then the rest: **she came straight out of the water; a wetsuit peeled to the waist; a bikini top; she argued ¶31–38 for an hour and a half; [verdict-gated] who took it.** Then: **"She kept her hands to herself."** — said by him, before Lena can ask (he learned last week what she'd ask; he gives it to her so she doesn't have to — and the Knowledge Auditor notes it's the condition's subject without the condition's words).
2. **Lena goes formal and polite** — her hurt register (bible §7: "When hurt she goes formal and polite, which is terrifying.") — **"Thank you for telling me."** — the door half shut behind her, KEEP OUT in Laney's capitals, the smell of fig and frying through the gap, flour to her elbows. **The wetsuit is worse than the gown and she knows it** (the gown was a joke; this is what Skye looks like). **She doesn't say so.** No joke; no "the actress"; no Skye's name. **One question, a new one:** **"Was she good?"** (meaning the argument) — "Yes." — "Good." (sharp, quiet — she respects an opponent; she hates that she does.)
3. **She is not unkind and she does not make it a thing.** She does not forbid, threaten, cry, or restate the condition. The not-calm is in **one** place (#114: infer): the oven mitt in her hand, which she puts on and takes off.
4. **She comes back from it on the page** — within the scene; that's the step on the ladder (§2): the first hurt that isn't a fight, and its mending in under a minute.

### 7.2 The choice `s2e22_door` (key every option; cue on [Candor] only)
- **[Candor]** "You went polite on me." — "I did." — a breath. "Give me a second to come back from it." — she takes the second, visibly, and comes back: "There. I'm back." — and kisses him, deliberately, with an oven mitt on one hand · `rel.lena.trust +1`, `soul.honesty +1` · cue **"Lena will remember that."** · Key: *"When you told Lena about Skye's second Wednesday and she went polite on you, you told her so, and she came back."*
- **[Sincerity]** "It's Wednesday. That's all it is." — "I know what it is." (quiet, not sharp) — then, softer: "I know what it is, Jack." — and she kisses him, short · `rel.lena.warmth +1` · Key: *"…you told her it was only Wednesday, and she said she knew."*
- **[Restraint]** *Wait. Let her come back on her own.* — the oven mitt on, off; then: "Friday is still mine." — she kisses his cheek and goes back in · `trait.control +1` · Key: *"…you waited until she came back on her own."*
*(Not pasted under every option (tic #291/#298): each response differs. **Then the FORK `s2e22_wed`** — [Theo] *Theo's texted twice from a party on the bluff.* / [Kevin] *Laney's rota is on Kevin's door; somebody should eat one of those casseroles with him.* — keys: "After telling Lena, you went to find Theo at a St. Patrick's party on the bluff." / "After telling Lena, you went to eat a casserole with Kevin.")*

### 7.3 Length and register
≈750. The quietest scene of the week. No music. No bystander (Laney's humming from inside, at most). It ends on her, not on the door.

## 8. THE VOTE — THE SPEC (s21, Sun Mar 21, 7:35–8:10 p.m. PDT, the Fishbowl; direct opposition as sport; the Voice Editor checks "no strawmen")
### 8.1 Rules
1. **Public record only** [R]: Sunday afternoon (Eastern) Stupak and his bloc announce they'll vote yes after the President agrees to an **executive order** on abortion funding; the House passes the **Senate bill** that night, **219–212** (≈10:45–10:50 p.m. Eastern / **≈7:45 p.m. Pacific**; the sheet's ~8:30 p.m. PDT is the session's end — §15.1), then the reconciliation fixes; the bishops (USCCB) opposed the final bill; the Catholic Health Association and the women religious' letter (Wed Mar 17) supported it. **No real person quoted** — not the Speaker, not Stupak, not the shout from the floor ("baby killer," Rep. Neugebauer — do not reproduce; at most "somebody shouted from the Republican side and apologized the next day" [R]).
2. **Both furious, differently** (bible map): **Lena** — a pro-life Catholic Worker Democrat who wanted the coverage *and* the Stupak language, who believed Stupak in November and feels the executive order is "a press release with a seal on it," betrayed by her own party and torn between the nuns and the bishops, glad for the millions and sick about the price; **Jack** — a Rust Belt law-and-order conservative against the bill on its merits (federal reach, the cost, the funding language), who called it Wednesday morning (s06): **"Told you."** **Neither is a strawman; each grants the other's best point at least once on some option.**
3. **The ensemble** (one sentence each, at the moment it matters; tic #278): **Margot** at the coffee table with a laptop for the *Tide* ("I'm writing *historic* in brackets until I decide how I feel"); **Theo** running a book on the margin out of the Oscar coffee can (Theo is apolitical and delighted); **Kevin**, the pre-med, quietly for the bill for the reason he says once (his mother's pharmacy, Fremont, the uninsured at the counter) — and not a word more; **Laney** furious at everyone on principle, then crying a little at the count for no reason she'll name; Caleb and Yusuf optional (Yusuf: one engineering metaphor).
4. **Frank** (§8.4) — the union, pro-life, laid-off Kennedy Democrat — torn like Lena, which Lena enjoys enormously ("Your *father* agrees with me").
5. **It ends in affection, not a truce** — the argument is the date-that-isn't; she kisses him to end a sentence she's losing, or he concedes one to end one he's winning (the option decides); then her step (s22).

### 8.2 The choice `s2e22_vote` (key every option; no cue)
- **[Press]** *An executive order can be rescinded with a pen. You can't vote for a law on a promise that can be torn up.* — Lena: "I *know*. That's why I'm *angry*, Costigan, not because you're right." · `trait.candor +1` · Key: *"When the House passed the health-care bill, you told Lena an executive order could be torn up with a pen, and she said that was exactly why she was angry."*
- **[Concede]** "Thirty million people get a doctor. That's not nothing." — Lena, stopped: "No. It isn't." — and she's angrier, now, at having to hold both · `rel.lena.trust +1` · Key: *"…you granted her that thirty million people getting a doctor wasn't nothing."* [R: the coverage figure as reported then]
- **[Wit]** "Your party just cut a deal with a Catholic from Michigan over a piece of paper. Welcome to my world." — "*Your* world has *Ohio* in it." · `rel.lena.warmth +1` · Key: *"…you welcomed her to your world, and she reminded you it had Ohio in it."*
- **[Sincerity]** "I hate that you got betrayed by people you voted for. I'd hate it if it were mine." — she looks at him a long time; "It *will* be yours one day. Remember you said that." (the long arc #47: both politically homeless, later — a seed, no prophecy) · `rel.lena.trust +1` · Key: *"…you told her you hated that she'd been betrayed by people she voted for."*

### 8.3 The Gospel in the room (never aimed)
Nobody mentions John 8 at the vote. **Once**, on her step after (s22), she says something about stones — or doesn't; the Writer's call. Never a sermon.

### 8.4 Frank's text (s22, 9:20 p.m. PDT, 12:20 Eastern — he stayed up)
ALL CAPS, e.g.: *PASSED. YOUR MOTHER SAYS GOOD. I SAY WE'LL SEE. STUPAK SHOULD HAVE HELD.* — Lena reads it over his arm: "Your father agrees with me." — "My father agrees with my mother." — (no fight; Frank's three texts this week are a set: St. Patrick's, the birthday, the vote).

- **Lena knows:** the campaign and session 1 (E21); **session 2 — the wetsuit, the bikini top, the length, who won, "She kept her hands to herself."** (s08, from Jack, the same day); **Skye knows his birthday and texted** (s14, shown); Danny's voice (`danny_met_lena`); what Ma told her Monday (the soup; Nonna's table; [Ma's line] "Danny was always Joseph" — **Lena may or may not have heard that from Ma; the Writer keeps it out of Lena's mouth** — she hears Danny's "That was always me" on [Sincerity] only if she's holding the phone, and doesn't ask). **She does not hear** Skye's own words; Osei's or Theo's advice; Hale's [hale] office talk; Kevin's 420 talk.
- **Laney knows:** Kevin and Clare — **from a Newman soprano, Tue (her route said in s05)**, not from Jack; that Jack kept it. Lena's plan (she's in it). Jack's birthday wall (she reads it; Lena can't — Facebook Lent).
- **Theo [theo]:** learns the campaign (first time on this save), two sessions, the gown and the wetsuit; that Lena was told first both times. **Not the condition.** 🔒 Nothing shows prior knowledge. [kevin path] Theo learns nothing of the campaign on the page; at the altar he's the innkeeper and nothing more.
- **Kevin [kevin]:** knows Jack didn't tell Laney (she told him who did). Knows nothing of the campaign.
- **Osei:** blesses the altar; knows the birthday; knows the campaign (E21 [osei]) — says nothing of it this week.
- **Hale [hale]:** hears it was Jack's birthday (the Fishbowl's noise; Lena's cookies "from the opposition"); [ohio candor] hears "It's being argued. Wednesdays." — and nothing more. Hale knows nothing of Skye.
- **Ma:** Lena called her Monday Mar 15 (the route for the soup and Nonna's table; Ma tells Jack she was called, not what for). **Ma told Danny** on his Wednesday call (off page) — Danny's route. **Nonna:** mailed the fave to Lena (her answer to "*Lei lo sa?*"). **Frank, Gina:** the birthday; Gina from Ma.
- **Danny:** knows from Ma that "the veil girl's doing the table"; learns Margot played Joseph; [sincerity] hears the table from Lena. 🔒 "That was always me." — Danny's; never explained to anyone.
- **Mémère:** told by Lena at Christmas 2009 (new canon, s17) — **resolves knowledge.md's "who told Mémère" (unresolved since S2E17)**. Celeste: mailed the cuccidati (Lena asked).
- **Marcus:** saw the box (Thu); reads Nonna's T.

## 15. PERIOD GUARDRAILS (the Researcher appends results to `period/s2e22-mar15-21-2010.md` §8; the plan leans only on what it confirms)

  - **§2 Fri Mar 19 — "a solemnity displaces Lenten abstinence in practice by dispensation (US bishops commonly allow…)": CORRECT IT.** No dispensation is needed: **Canon 1251 itself exempts a solemnity falling on a Friday** from the Friday abstinence (and from the US Lenten-Friday abstinence, which follows the same canon). This plan's §0 ruling stands; the Researcher appends the canon's wording to the sheet's §8 and strikes "by dispensation." Lena's line ("Canon twelve fifty-one.
